#2183. Birmingham City set to welcome back James Beadle for Stoke City clash, but doubts remain over six-goal star

Birmingham City have gone into the November international break top of the Championship. BCFC have been really impressive to start the year, and have continued winning games, even with some injuries to key players.

Chris Davies’ side have gone the their last two games without goalkeeper James Beadle and top-scorer August Priske. However, as reported by Birmingham Live, Davies has provided an update on the pairs fitness going into the internationals.

“James (Beadle) definitely will be (back),” Davies confirmed but he couldn’t be so sure on Priske. “August (Priske) did everything to get ready but he couldn’t,” the Blues manager said.

“I presume he is going to make more strides in the next week or so. I’d hope he was back after the international break but I couldn’t be definite on that because it’s how it settles down.”

Davies also provided an update on Marc Leonard who is not part of the Birmingham City squad after being omitted at the start of the season following an Achilles injury. The BCFC boss said he is looking to be back in action after the New Year.

Both Beadle and Priske could return for Birmingham City’s trip to Stoke City on 21 November, but the striker’s availability would be the bigger boost.

Beadle is the established first-choice goalkeeper after missing the wins over Millwall and Swansea with a knock. Davies has confirmed he will be available for Stoke, although Ryan Allsop has impressed in his absence.

Priske’s return is less certain, but potentially more significant. The forward provides their main focal point in attack. Davies is hopeful the international break will give him enough time to recover, but has stopped short of guaranteeing his availability.

While BCFC have continued scoring without him, having their main goal threat back for such an important away fixture would be a considerable boost.

Priske has been a key player for Birmingham City this season and has really come into his own after a slow start last term. However, his injury has not slowed up the Blues as many might’ve thought.

Davies has adjusted his side well and in Priske’s absence, BCFC have still been an attacking threat. Max Bird has come into the side as part of the front four with great success.

Jay Stansfield has also found his scoring touch with three goals in his last two games. The Blues have adjusted very well to Priske’s absence, and while they were prefer to have their top-scorer in action, the club have shown they are still strong enough without him.

#2182. Glasgow biotech teams up with Nottingham Forest to trial injury risk blood test

A Glasgow biotech company has teamed up with Premier League club Nottingham Forest to investigate whether blood tests could help identify injury risks in professional footballers before they happen.

Entourage, based at Kelvin Campus, is conducting a 12-week study involving the club’s 25-player men’s B team, with support from Scottish Enterprise.

The Entourage Team. (Image: Martin Shields)

The research will use plasma proteomics, the analysis of proteins in blood, to monitor players’ biological responses to training, matches and recovery.

Players will provide blood samples twice a week, allowing researchers to analyse thousands of proteins and track individual responses to physical demands, injuries and recovery.

By combining the results with existing training, workload and clinical information, the team hopes to identify protein patterns that could indicate a greater risk of non-contact soft tissue injuries.

The trial is described as the first high-frequency plasma proteomic study involving a professional sports team.

A blood sample being collected. (Image: Supplied)

The research comes amid concerns about the impact of hamstring injuries in elite football. UEFA figures for the 2021/22 season found they accounted for one in five days of injury absence across elite European football, double the rate recorded two decades earlier.

Entourage’s technology focuses on using protein analysis to better understand inflammation, immune function and recovery. The company is also building a reference dataset for elite athletes, supported by its research centre at Kelvin Campus.

The findings could help clubs develop more personalised training and recovery plans based on players’ individual biological responses.

The company also sees potential applications for its technology beyond sport, including the early detection of conditions such as cardiovascular disease, diabetes and Alzheimer’s.

Entourage lab technician Matthew Mitchell prepares a blood sample for testing in their lab. (Image: Elaine Livingstone)

Entourage was co-founded by Professor Patrick Gunning, Dr Taleb Sedghi and Dr Mark Lindsay and launched with a $5 million pre-seed funding round.

Professor Gunning previously co-founded Dunad Therapeutics and helped secure a $1.3 billion partnership with Novartis.

The company plans to report its findings to Nottingham Forest at the end of the 12-week study.

Voluntary Health Scotland relaunches as Health Creation Scotland to tackle inequality

Century-old foundation stone restored and relocated at Aberdeen children's hospital

Aberdeen 'set' to sack Stephen Robinson as axe looms at Pittodrie

Professor Patrick Gunning, chief executive and co-founder of Entourage, said:"One of the questions we are trying to answer is whether there are signals in the blood that could indicate an increased risk of a non-contact injury.

"That is something we need to investigate through the study."

Dimitrios Kalogiannidis, head of medical at Nottingham Forest, said: "We are deeply committed to evidence-led player care.

"We believe in the importance of building a more robust biological profile of each player, so we’re pleased to be taking part in this research study that could benefit Nottingham Forest and the wider game for years to come."

Entourage’s chief executive and co-founder, Professor Patrick Gunning pictured with lab scientist Daniel Poloske. (Image: Elaine Livingstone)

Dr Mark Lindsay, co-founder of Entourage, said: "We're moving soft-tissue care from estimation to measurement.

"We read the biology of the injury, precision directives guide every stage of treatment, and return to play is decided by the data.

"You can't manage what you can't measure."

#2181. Calciomercato no stop - Indiscrezioni, trattative e retroscena del 10 novembre

Accostato alla Roma nelle scorse settimane, l'attaccante esterno del Club Brugge Carlos Forbs pare non essere uscito dai radar di D'Amico, ma ha tanti ammiratori in giro per l'Europa. Secondo le informazioni pubblicate dal Sun infatti, la società giallorossa lo segue insieme a Tottenham, Arsenal, Manchester United e Fiorentina. Non si tratta però di un'operazione da fare nel corso della sessione invernale di mercato, il club belga infatti lo cederà solamente la prossima estate, come da prassi per quanto riguarda il mercato in uscita del club belga.

Tra Noa Lang e la tifoseria del Napoli la frattura è ormai evidente. Contro la Lazio, al Maradona, l’esterno olandese ha cercato il sostegno della gente portandosi le mani alle orecchie, in un gesto simile a un "non vi sento". La risposta della curva, però, è stata all'insegna... dei fischi. I tifosi non gli hanno perdonato quanto accaduto a Porto: dopo la sconfitta per 4-1, Lang aveva lasciato infatti il campo senza salutare il settore ospiti. Da allora il rapporto tra queste due componenti del mondo partenopeo si è profondamente incrinato. Il Napoli ha assoluta necessità di intervenire sul mercato e questo a prescindere dai risultati che verranno conseguiti da qui a fine dicembre. Il centrocampo è il settore nel quale il direttore sportivo Manna è pronto a intervenire con decisione vista la situazione di incertezza legata al futuro di Anguissa e Lobotka. Coulibaly del Monaco è la primissima scelta, ma la trattativa come detto più volte non è certo semplice visto che per il classe 2004 il club del Principato chiede tantissimo. Sempre rimanendo al Monaco piace Camara, ma il discorso anche in questo caso è complicato. E allora i riflettori del Napoli sono puntati anche su altri giocatori. Una pista da non sottovalutare è quella che porta ad Adem Zorgane dell’Union Saint-Gilloise, che negli ultimi anni è diventata una società in grado di mettere in mostra tanti talenti.

Mario Hermoso è diventato uno dei simboli della Roma di Gasperini. Lo spagnolo classe 1995 interpreta il ruolo di difensore in maniera moderna e aggressiva, anticipando gli avversari e partecipando costantemente alla manovra offensiva. Da quando lavora con Gasp ha realizzato sette reti, tre delle quali in questa stagione. Numeri che testimoniano una trasformazione sorprendente per un giocatore cresciuto presso la scuola del Cholo Simeone. L’attuale accordo scadrà il prossimo 30 giugno, ma la volontà di proseguire insieme è reciproca. Le parti sarebbero vicine a un’intesa fino al 2029, con opzione per il 2030. Hermoso sarebbe inoltre disposto ad accettare una riduzione dell’ingaggio pur di continuare la propria avventura nella Capitale.

Il dado è tratto in casa Sassuolo, la società neroverde ha deciso di esonerare Alberto Aquilani e ora la notizia è anche ufficiale. Si legge, nel comunicato ufficiale degli emiliani: "L’U.S. Sassuolo Calcio comunica di aver sollevato l’allenatore Alberto Aquilani e il suo staff dai rispettivi incarichi. La Società ringrazia il tecnico e i suoi collaboratori per l’impegno profuso e la serietà dimostrata alla guida dei neroverdi e augura loro le migliori fortune per il futuro".

Il Sassuolo è pronto a stringere sul profilo di Roberto D'Aversa come suo prossimo allenatore. Vi avevamo dato conto della preferenza dei neroverdi che si stava direzionando verso il tecnico abruzzese e anche adesso raccogliamo conferme. Pronto per l'ex Torino, Empoli e Lecce tra le altre un contratto fino al termine della stagione, al 30 giugno 2027, con opzione per andare avanti assieme un altro anno.

Il Sassuolo ha il suo nuovo allenatore. Roberto D’Aversa firmerà il suo contratto con il club neroverde fino a giugno con opzione. In sostanza manca solo l’annuncio ufficiale visto che gli accordi verbali sono stati tutti raggiunti nel tardo pomeriggio. Insomma D’Aversa è pronto a cominciare questa sua nuova avventura dopo l’esperienza molto positiva nel finale della scorsa stagione alla guida del Torino. Il tecnico si metterà subito a lavoro nelle prossime ore cercando di sfruttare al meglio questa sosta, anche se diversi giocatori saranno impegnati con le rispettive Nazionali.

Il futuro di Julian Alvarez e Martin Zubimendi potrebbe incrociarsi. Secondo alcune indiscrezioni provenienti dalla Spagna, Arsenal e Atletico Madrid starebbero valutando una doppia operazione che coinvolgerebbe due giocatori arrivati all'estate con grandi aspettative ma protagonisti di momenti complicati. L'attaccante argentino aveva manifestato in estate la volontà di lasciare l'Atletico per trasferirsi al Barcellona, ma il club madrileno ha bloccato la sua partenza. L'inizio di stagione, però, non è stato quello sperato: appena due gol in 12 presenze per la squadra di Diego Simeone, dopo i 20 realizzati in 49 partite nella scorsa annata. Nonostante il momento difficile, il tecnico argentino continua a difenderlo. Dopo la sconfitta contro il Barcellona, Simeone ha ribadito la necessità di ritrovare "il miglior Julian", convinto che l'attaccante possa tornare decisivo. L'Arsenal resta interessato al classe 2000, individuato come possibile rinforzo offensivo, anche se già in estate l'ex River aveva rifiutato il trasferimento in Premier.

Nessun futuro al Fenerbahçe per Francesco Totti, almeno secondo quanto comunicato ufficialmente dal club turco. Nelle ultime ore, il quotidiano Sözcü aveva rilanciato l'ipotesi di un ingresso dell'ex capitano della Roma nella dirigenza sportiva della società di Istanbul. Una suggestione che aveva rapidamente attirato l'attenzione dei media locali, anche alla luce del momento complicato attraversato dal Fenerbahçe. Totti, attualmente senza incarichi ufficiali nel mondo del calcio, era stato indicato come una possibile figura di riferimento per rilanciare il progetto sportivo gialloblù. La società, però, ha deciso di intervenire direttamente per smentire la ricostruzione.

Il Lione ha ufficializzato l'ingaggio di Carlos Vicens come nuovo allenatore della prima squadra. La società francese ha spiegato di aver individuato nel tecnico spagnolo il profilo ideale per dare continuità al progetto sportivo, puntando su una filosofia di gioco, un metodo di lavoro e una particolare attenzione alla crescita dei calciatori pienamente in linea con gli obiettivi del club.

Raúl González Blanco torna in panchina. L'ex attaccante e leggenda del Real Madrid è ufficialmente il nuovo allenatore del Real Oviedo, formazione che nella passata stagione militava nella Liga e che attualmente occupa il nono posto nella seconda divisione spagnola. Per il tecnico quarantanovenne si tratta di una nuova esperienza professionale dopo il lungo percorso all'interno del settore giovanile dei Blancos, culminato con la guida del Real Madrid Castilla fino al 2025.

Il Real Madrid è pronto a mettere le mani su uno dei talenti più promettenti del calcio europeo. I blancos avrebbero infatti vinto la concorrenza di Barcellona, Paris Saint-Germain e Bayern Monaco per JJ Gabriel, sedicenne del Manchester United considerato un potenziale fuoriclasse. Dopo mesi di contatti e trattative, l'operazione sarebbe ormai vicina alla conclusione, con il giocatore pronto ad annunciare la propria destinazione nei prossimi giorni. I Red Devils hanno tentato in ogni modo di trattenerlo, arrivando persino a convocarlo in prima squadra per una partita di coppa quando aveva appena 15 anni. Una mossa che, però, non è bastata a modificare la sua decisione.

#2180. Blackburn Rovers, Birmingham City track Chelsea starlet after Tony Mowbray snubbed summer loan deal

Blackburn Rovers and Birmingham City have both performed positively over the first quarter of the 2026/27 campaign, but they’ll have eyes on the January transfer window as an opportunity to strengthen the ranks further. Now, TEAMtalk has claimed one player of interest to both clubs is Kendry Paez.

The report states that Chelsea are actively looking for potential loan destinations for Ecuadorian prospect Paez. Rovers and the Blues are keen, but the 19-year-old also has admirers at Celtic and Trabzonspor, and he was eyed by Belgium’s KV Mechelen in the summer.

Paez was on the radar at Ewood Park in the last transfer window as was put to Tony Mowbray as a potential signing. However, the boss snubbed the deal, and the attacking midfielder has remained with Chelsea instead.

Paez was viewed as one of South America’s most exciting talents when he officially joined Chelsea in 2025. However, loans at RC Strasbourg and River Plate have not yielded the success hoped.

Blackburn Rovers were lined up as a potential destination in Paez’s bid to settle into English football, but after Mowbray opted against bringing him in, he has only played U21s football.

Paez has made six appearances across the Premier League 2 and EFL Trophy. He provided an assist in a 2-1 defeat to Wycombe Wanderers in September and played a little over an hour in a 2-0 triumph over Reading in the same cup competitions in October.

At 19, the Chelsea starlet is still young and has plenty of time to come good in England. But, Paez has struggled to impact games the way he did in his emergence in Ecuador with Independiente del Valle, so it remains to be seen if a half-season loan to the Championship can breathe life into his career again.

This applies to Birmingham City more given that they are the current Championship leaders, but clubs have to be careful with loan deals in the winter. Recruitment staff and managers alike need to be wary of how new signings will impact a thriving squad.

Loan players come in with a desire to play, but it can be tough to find opportunities in a thriving team. If that were to happen to Paez, it wouldn’t help his development and could bring players off the pitch too.

Blackburn Rovers aren’t in quite the same position as they’re 13th, but Mowbray has harnessed some strong squad spirit. The fact the boss has already snubbed the loan signing of Paez would raise questions over a winter deal for his services too.

Time will tell what comes of Paez’s winter transfer fate as Chelsea look for potential destinations.

#2178. Struggling £26m West Ham man eyed for escape transfer to South American giants

West Ham signed Taty Castellanos for a deal worth around £26m in January as they bid to salvage their Premier League status. WHUFC came up short despite their new striker netting six goals in 18 Premier League games, but his form has not continued in the Championship.

Now, a report from Argentine news outlet El Intransigente says Castellanos could find an escape route back to his home country. River Plate are keen on signing a new striker and the Hammers man sits atop their shortlist of targets.

Despite being from Argentina, Castellanos has never played senior football in his native. He has played in Chile, Uruguay, Spain and the United States, as well as in England with West Ham.

West Ham have not had an easy time in the Championship, dropping to 9th after Saturday’s loss to Blackburn Rovers. However, with 37 goals scored in 15 matches, they are still the league’s highest-scoring team by far.

Despite this, and his pedigree at a higher level, Castellanos has not found form in front of goal. He started the competitive campaign with a Carabao Cup brace against Portsmouth and scored in the same competition against Southampton in August too.

But, the 28-year-old has found the back of the net just once in the Championship, that being in the 6-0 home thumping of Wrexham. Castellanos has chipped in with a strong tally of five assists, but a total of 3.46 xG shows how he is underperforming in terms of goalscoring (FotMob).

It has seen him drop from the WHUFC starting lineup for four of the last six league games. Now, it brings questions over his future as River Plate lurk.

As highlighted, West Ham are scoring plenty of goals. However, they’re not coming from the strikers.

Of the natural no. 9’s on the books, Joel Piroe is the top scorer on four. Pablo earns praise for his physicality and work rate but two goals in 28 career games for WHUFC shows he leaves a lot to be desired in front of goal.

By selling Castellanos, the Hammers can raise funds and free up space in the squad for a new talisman to join. They can ease the burden on Piroe while Pablo can mainly be used in the impact substitute role he tends to be better in.

It could breathe new life into Scott Parker’s attack with clear threats out wide and in midfield like Jarrod Bowen, Edvin Austbo, Manor Solomon, Divine Mukasa and Mohamadou Kante.

#2177. Kim Hellberg footage after Middlesbrough's draw v Watford is becoming a talking point

Middlesbrough dropped points and failed to score for the second time this week on Saturday at Watford, and Boro boss Kim Hellberg was seen visibly frustrated after the final whistle.

The draw at Vicarage Road saw Middlesbrough relinquish the top spot in the Championship ahead of the November international break, with Birmingham City climbing above them on goal difference.

It's not how Boro would have wanted to sign off this mini block of fixtures between international breaks, and the performance against Watford, especially, would have provided plenty of food for thought in the dugout.

Pick any two players. Send the code, and your mate enters it here to play the same pair.

Indeed, this was the second successive game without leading talisman and Championship top scorer Will Lankshear due to injury, and it's almost universally agreed that if the 21-year-old was leading the line on Saturday, they'd have cruised to victory, as Boro had 18 shots, 34 touches in Watford's box and four big chances, but couldn't score.

Middlesbrough boss Kim Hellberg has shown how passionate he is about the game in the 12 months he's spent in charge at the Riverside. Therefore, it was clear that a result like this, following such a dominant performance, was going to affect him.

After the game, the Swede was filmed staring blankly at the ground for around half a minute, just reflecting on the game, and Boro fans have reacted accordingly.

Some have made jokey comments about the state of the frontline when Lankshear is injured. One Boro fan took it upon themselves to caption the video, "What having Kyle Joseph as an attacking option does to man." Another commented, "Please god make Will be fit, please god make Will be fit…..🙏."

"He’s got Kyle Joseph depression," was also among the reactions to the video.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top nine toughest fanbases in the EFL Championship right now from least to most

The majority of comments in response to Hellberg's post-match reaction were about how invested he is in the game, though, and Middlesbrough fans know how lucky they are to have the Swede in charge.

"You can just tell this isn’t performative. Like the fist bumps and the celebrations when we win, or the tears during the play-off debacle last season, Kim ‘gets it’, he’s immersed himself into the #Boro way. Never seen a manager so invested in us," one Boro fan tweeted.

"Carries it all, the highs and lows just like the fans. “A good leader leads the people from above them. A great leader leads the people from within them," was also among the comments, as was, "The reaction of a man who puts the weight of the world on his own shoulders #UTB."

"Very powerful, but also makes me want to give him a hug and tell him he's one of my favourite ever Boro gaffers and everything will be alright," someone commented in response to the video, showcasing the love and respect Boro fans have towards their head coach.

This is the reaction of a man spent after a disappointing result, having coached what was effectively the perfect game.

Plenty of head coaches nowadays are focused on the journey, the performance levels and good underlying numbers, and Hellberg is definitely one of those, but he also recognises how important it is to win games, too.

As one Boro fan mentioned, he carries the weight of expectation amongst Middlesbrough fans, who are desperate to return to the Premier League after a decade away, and you can imagine he takes not winning as seriously as some of those in the stands do, too.

It's reactions like this that are why many are touting Hellberg to become a top-flight manager in the near future, and Boro supporters hope it'll be with them.

However, it's hard to ignore the fact that, while Lankshear sits on the sidelines, Middlesbrough struggles to score goals and win games.

Boro's wasteful nature in front of goal dominated the narrative surrounding them in the final few months of last season as they slipped out of the running for automatic promotion and into the play-offs, and they could have been out of sight in the first half of their play-off semi-final first leg against Southampton if not for poor finishing, too.

Lankshear has papered over those cracks, as he's been brilliant at finishing the plenty of chances Middlesbrough create in a game, but the alarm bells are starting to ring without him.

Do you think Middlesbrough can turn their finishing troubles around without Will Lankshear in the side? Let us know in the comments.

Hopefully, the 21-year-old will be back after the international break and won't suffer any more injury setbacks, as that could be the difference between promotion and some grim de ja vu.

It's something Hellberg will be mulling over for the next two weeks, and it seemingly started immediately after the final whistle at Watford.

#2175. National League clubs interested as 31-year-old leaves Millwall by mutual consent

Millwall have had experienced goalkeeper Joel Coleman on the books as cover since the summer of 2025. He is yet to make an appearance for the Lions and was left out of their 25-man EFL squad for the ongoing campaign.

Now, writing on X, reporter Pete O’Rourke states that Coleman and the club have come to an agreement over the mutual termination of his contract. It frees the 31-year-old to depart and find a new team outside of the transfer window, and there are National League sides keen on the shot-stopper.

Coleman has played the majority of football across his career in League One, but he has appeared in League Two and the Championship too. He was in three matchday squads for Alex Neil‘s side this season prior to his omission.

Including Coleman, Millwall have had five goalkeepers on their books this season. Even with Lukas Jensen and Filip Marschall cast to the sidelines through injury, the former Bolton Wanderers and Huddersfield Town man has been third choice behind Max Crocombe and George Evans for the most part.

Injuries can bring opportunities for the backup ‘keepers, but the Lions’ depth in this position means it would take a serious crisis, dismal form from several players or both for Coleman to come into contention for minutes in Neil’s side.

It means the Championship club can afford to let him go mid-season without signing a replacement. Coleman joined on a free transfer as well, so they’re not taking a big loss in terminating his contract either.

It remains to be seen just where he winds up after his contract end, but this move should benefit Coleman.

Coleman has spent much of his career as a backup goalkeeper, but his minutes have dried up in recent times especially. He never played for Millwall and he didn’t appear in his final season at Bolton Wanderers in 2024/25 either.

Having kept training, the Bolton-born shot-stopper will hope he can move to a National League club and quickly become a hit. He’ll have plenty of experience and pedigree to offer so could become an influential asset on and off the pitch.

Coleman’s best season came as he broke through at Oldham Athletic, keeping 12 clean sheets and conceding 35 goals in 32 League One appearances across the 2015/16 season. He will hope to tap back into that form in the bid to become a starting shot-stopper again.

#2174. West Ham face Taty Castellanos, River Plate transfer exit threat

As West Ham continue to fall short of rekindling their fortunes despite Scott Parker's recent appointment, the possibility of Taty Castellanos departing the London Stadium in the coming months has now emerged in a development that will spark mixed reactions from Hammers supporters.

West Ham's return to the Championship following 14 successive seasons of solid Premier League standing is not going to plan right now, with the East Londoners appearing increasingly unlikely to seal a previously-expected top-flight return at the very first time of asking as champions.

A mixed run of form featuring consecutive home defeats at home to a pair of promotion rivals in Southampton and West Bromwich Albion ultimately ended Nuno Espirito Santo's unpopular tenure in charge, and the Portuguese boss has been swiftly succeeded by former Hammers midfielder Parker.

The ex-Bournemouth and Fulham boss, having already achieved three promotions from the second-tier with as many clubs after most recently guiding Burnley back to the promised land in 2025 before his dismissal towards the end of the previous campaign, has encountered a challenging return to the club thus far, though, taking just one point from his opening two matches.

Last Wednesday's thrilling 3-3 comeback draw at Cardiff City after overturning a three-goal defecit at half-time was followed up by a disappointing 2-1 defeat away to Blackburn Rovers over the weekend, and with the Hammers yet to enjoy the anticipated upturn under Parker, they now face the threat of losing one of their big-name performers once the January window rolls around.

Castellanos has endured a particularly difficult and underwhelming introduction to the Championship, which the Argentine forward was expected to light up after his mid-season arrival back in January preceded encouraging goalscoring form in spite of his side's ultimate failure to dodge the drop.

Six goals from 18 Premier League appearances means that the current return of just one strike across 14 second-tier fixtures - nine of which have been starts - is not what fans were anticipating, and they had presented vocal criticism towards the divisive 28-year-old for a number of weeks before Parker hauled the attacker off at the interval against the Bluebirds and then dropped his name out of the starting line-up at Ewood Park.

It has now emerged that Castellanos could leave East London as early as January, with a fresh report from Argentine-based outlet El Intransigente claiming strong long-term interest from River Plate.

As per the update, River Plate have held interest in the two-cap Argentine international's services in recent windows and he represents a "top target" in the eyes of manager Leonardo Ponzio.

Blackburn Rovers captain Todd Cantwell has been among those reacting to the club's dig towards Scott Parker's West Ham after Saturday's 2-1 victory

It's said that the Buenos Aries-based giants feel strongly about Castellano's profile and international experience, with the striker having risen to prominence outside of his native Argentina in Uruguay with Montevideo City Torque before enjoying productive spells at New York City, Girona and Lazio ahead of joining West Ham earlier this year for a reported £26 million figure on a four-and-a-half year contract.

West Ham supporters have grown increasingly critical of Castellanos and his long-term future under Parker remains unclear at this moment in time, although a fresh hurdle could prevent River Plate from sealing the deal.

River Plate have been presented with a significant obstacle to overcome if they intend on returning Castellanos to his homeland, as it's a move which wouldn't come cheaply for the 38-time Primera Division champions.

Indeed, the report has added that the salary Castellanos earns at the London Stadium is the main challenge for River Plate right now, leaving the club to weigh up alternative options such as acquiring the sought-after frontman on loan or even purchasing a percentage of his rights.

Both of those options, of course, would require the prior approval of West Ham, whose own stance on potentially parting ways with the ex-Lazio striker just a year after his arrival with a club likely unable to recoup the figure of purchase is also not yet known.

It's noted that Castellanos' lengthy contract with the Championship promotion hopefuls puts Parker's side in a comfortable negotiating decision, and hold all the cards as River Plate will not be able to wrap up any deal without their support.

Interestingly, however, the update also makes reference to previous interest elsewhere, reminding that Castellanos had reportedly wished to leave West Ham in the summer amid loan interest from Seville-based rivals Real Betis and Sevilla that never materialised.

The London Stadium faithful remain very much divided on Castellanos at the moment, with some making their minds up on the out-of-form forward and others willing to persist with further patience.

Prior claims of a January exit if the price is met at the end of the previous month saw some West Ham fans refuse to hold back on their feelings about Castellanos' future, and this one fan believes that his hapless form in the Championship would make for no great loss if he was to depart.

Another would be happy to see the Hammers striker leave at any cost, even if the club themselves surely won't take that stance.

Castellanos has left some frustrated for reasons beyond his inability to find the back of the net on a regular basis, too, and having formerly scored at a relatively-prolific rate leading the line for a side relegated from the Premier League, his work-rate and application have been called into question.

His display in the aforementioned draw at Cardiff drew particular criticism, and West Ham were a much more fluid, functional and threatening outfit once the Argentine was withdrawn for Joel Piroe at the break.

Now, with the Suriname international scoring in successive encounters, it's unclear how Castellanos will force his way back into starting contention ahead of a positional rival with a historically-goal-laden hit-rate at this level.

West Ham fans, how do you feel about Taty Castellanos' future? Would you hold onto the striker, or is it time to cash in? What should his valuation be? Let us know your thoughts in the comments below!

But there are other fans who continue to stand by Castellanos, and considering how he fared at the highest level last time out, it's no great surprise that certain supporters still believe he can come good in the second-tier.

Either way, there are plenty of West Ham fans who, despite the criticism of the majority, do feel that the divisive striker would thrive in a system that emphasises chance creation under a manager willing to trust in his services and get the best out of an undeniably-talented striker with pedigree in more prestigious and higher-quality divisions.

This punter, meanwhile, reckons that cashing in on Castellanos would be a "stupid decision", but only time will tell to see what action West Ham decide to take and whether a deal can successfully be thrashed out with River Plate.

#2173. John McGlynn pinpoints key Falkirk star with 'sky's the limit' verdict

John McGlynn has pinpointed Jacob Murrell as Falkirk's key man as he reckons the "sky's the limit" for the forward.

The striker, 22, has taken the Scottish Premiership by storm since he arrived from DC United in the summer. Already with seven goals in just 11 appearances to his name, the US national has shone for the club.

Murrell added to his tally with a brilliant brace against Hearts on Sunday afternoon in their 5-2 hammering of the Jambos and McGlynn is confident he can go right to the top.

The attacker's form and profile mirrors former Bairns striker Barney Stewart who was the standout for his club last season before earning a £1.3million switch to West Bromwich Albion.

Falkirk's Jacob Murrell scores their side's third goal of the game but is ruled out due to VAR during the William Hill Premiership match at the Falkirk Stadium, Falkirk. Picture date: Sunday November 1, 2026. PA Photo. Photo credit should read: Steve (Image: Steve Welsh)

And when asked if Murrell might even have more potential than Stewart, McGlynn admitted he feels the former is a better 'all round' player than his predecessor.

"All round he has (got more to his game), all round he has," McGlynn said on Premier Sports' Scottish Football Social Club show. "Barney was a bit of a live wire, things would happen.

"A constructed striker. Jacob's all round a better striker actually and at 22, the sky's the limit to be honest with you. Anything could happen.

"Earlier in the season we played against Rangers at the Falkirk Stadium and he missed a couple of chances. You could almost replicate with Barney the year before against Celtic. He missed a couple of chances. Low and behold Barney went on to hit gold, starting scoring goals for fun.

"Jacob's now the same. Maybe there were doubts early on about Jacob but now he's really found his feet."

Falkirk's recruitment has been applauded in recent weeks following what was an unbelievable overhaul where a whopping 17 new faces entered the door this summer.

And for McGlynn it was a mammoth undertaking for himself and his scouts at the club, whom he credits for most of the hard work.

Allan Fraser and Paul Lennon joined the boss - who has the final say on signings - in identifying talent and McGlynn insists their old school approach has worked well for them.

He also revealed the staff are already planning for next summer with the January transfer window perhaps being used for one or two incomings and a heavy raft of outgoings.

"We're looking at the summer now because we know in January we're not gonna do an awful lot," McGlynn added. "But we're working on a couple of things.

"It may well be that we'll have to move a couple of players out as well because we've not got enough seats in the changing room for them! We are gonna have to move some on to bring another couple in.

"And we're looking longer term a little bit because the loan players we have done an amazing job for us last year.

"We don't have Jamestown Analytics, it's more old school. Allan (Fraser) is 67, has an eye for players, a spade is a spade, don't muck about, know what we're looking for."

#2172. Birmingham City, Celtic & Blackburn eye Chelsea man who could be 'better than Messi'

Blackburn Rovers and Birmingham City are both keen on a deal to sign Kendry Paez from Chelsea.

According to Teamtalk, the two Championship sides are both keen on the 19-year-old ahead of the January transfer window.

The teenager, who has been tipped to potentially be 'better than Messi' by ex-Ecuador international Carlos Tenorio, is currently a part of the first team squad at Stamford Bridge after returning early from a loan spell with River Plate.

However, he is not a part of Xabi Alonso’s plans at the moment, and has yet to play a single minute for the Spaniard this season.

The attacking-midfielder could be available in the new year, with the Premier League side seeking a loan move in order for Paez to gain valuable experience.

Chelsea signed the Ecuadorian in 2025 in a deal worth £17.3 million, but he has yet to make his debut for the club.

It’s believed that both Birmingham and Blackburn are keen on a deal to sign Paez when the winter market opens in January.

However, the pair will face competition from Scottish giants Celtic, who are also interested in signing the 19-year-old.

The playmaker previously spent time on loan with Strasbourg, with the French club owned by the same group as the Blues.

Jude and Jobe Bellingham have been named in Thomas Tuchel's senior Three Lions squad together for the first time

But he was only capable of making 15 appearances in Ligue 1, including four starts, scoring on just one occasion before going to River Plate January 2026.

It’s understood that Chelsea have been encouraged by his performances at underage level, and are now seeking a loan deal again in the upcoming January market.

Blackburn and Birmingham are both keen on a deal, but there are clubs from across Europe keeping tabs on the player’s progress, including Trabzonspor and KV Mechelen.

The Belgian side reportedly made an approach last summer, and are aiming to be at the front of the queue again in the new year.

Chris Davies’ side will be targeting promotion back to the Premier League in the second half of the campaign, while the Lancashire outfit will be aiming to break into the top eight.

Birmingham will return to action against Stoke City on 21 November, while Rovers will travel to face bitter rivals Burnley the following afternoon.

Paez wasn’t ready for top flight football in France last year, and his underwhelming performances reflected that.

But he’s now had time with Chelsea’s underage team to develop his game and better prepare for senior football, as he is still only 19 and moving from South America to Europe isn’t an easy transition.

If Paez can make that step up, then he could bring the kind of attacking flair and creativity in the second half of the season that can bolster a promotion push for either Birmingham or Blackburn.

#2171. Two EFL club takeovers collapse at final hour

Both AFC Wimbledon's and Bromley's proposed investment plans have hit a roadblock at the eleventh hour.

The League One duo were expected to struggle in the third tier this year. Bromley are in the division for the first time in their history, having only just been promoted into the EFL for the first time three years ago, while AFC Wimbledon are proudly fan-owned.

Ultimately, with the finances in football only increasing, plus some of the juggernauts in the division this year, such as Leicester City, Huddersfield Town and Sheffield Wednesday, who can afford to invest in the squad and the infrastructure at the club, their backs were always going to be against the wall.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top 25 most demanding fanbases in British football right now

It seemed as if they'd be given some financial help in the not-so-distant future, though. AFC Wimbledon were approached by a consortium led by ex-Chelsea defender Ryan Bertrand to take a minority 34% of the club's shares, while a consortium led by former Everton frontman Victor Anichebe was heavily linked with a takeover at Hayes Lane.

However, in recent updates, AFC Wimbledon have confirmed that the Sloane Street Capital investment group has withdrawn from their deal to invest up to £87 million over five years, one week ahead of what would have been an important fan vote to pass it.

Bertrand needed the financial backing of British technology expert Omar Mir to go through with the minority investment, but his 'investment priorities have changed,' according to a statement.

The collapse hasn't deterred the 37-year-old ex-England international, though, with it said that he, alongside his partners at Sloane Street Football, will be seeking fresh investment and returning to the Dons with a proposal on the same terms.

Meanwhile, Pete O'Rourke has reported that the African consortium's takeover of Bromley, fronted by Anichebe, is on the verge of collapse, which, to some, can be seen as a bullet dodged.

The Shoreline Limited group has been the main avenue of blame attributed to the state that National League side Gateshead are currently in.

They attempted to buy the North East club a couple of seasons ago, but after it failed, the group needed to sign waivers over money they claim they are owed, so the side currently at the bottom of the fifth tier can be taken over by another group and avoid the possibility of liquidation.

While not a concrete fact, it's strongly inferred that Andy Woodman's dismissal after five-and-a-half years in charge of Bromley, during which he won the FA Trophy and two promotions to the highest level the Ravens have played at, was down to the proposed takeover.

Many feel that the sacking of Woodman just 10 games into the League One campaign was in favour of a new brand of football that this new consortium wanted to see at Hayes Lane. Now, some feel that an unpopular dismissal was for nothing.

One fan claimed that the group had "turned Gateshead into near collapse," and "ruined 2 football clubs," after this breakdown.

Some are now worrying that, as with Gateshead, Shoreline Sports could end up suing the club over financial claims against shareholders, which could put any future investment into the club in jeopardy.

Ultimately, it's been quite a mess at Bromley in the opening stages of the season. Karl Robinson lost his first game in charge in the FA Cup against a Rotherham United side experiencing troubling times themselves in League Two.

This failed takeover will just create more confusion and uncertainty about the club's future, which will detract from what was set to be a historic campaign in the club's history.

In an ideal world, AFC Wimbledon wouldn't need any minority investment, and they'd be able to compete solely as a fan-run club.

The reality isn't that, though, and it's something that the Dons fans have come to accept if they are to break through the glass ceiling currently encasing their ambitions.

Do you think either AFC Wimbledon or Bromley will receive some investment before the end of 2026? Let us know in the comments.

As long as any funding doesn't turn into a full-blown takeover, that's the compromise that a club like AFC Wimbledon has to take, and this proposed 34% investment wouldn't have done so. Therefore, some Dons fans were actually looking forward to getting it over the line.

The news surrounding SSC pulling out is a disappointment, but there can be some optimism in the fact that Bertrand and co are still seeking investment routes into the club. Now all they have to do is wait to see if that comes.

Meanwhile, both sides will continue to punch above their weight and battle bravely against the third-tier drop this season.

#2170. Exclusive: Birmingham City & Middlesbrough plot transfer that will interest Leeds United

Birmingham City and Middlesbrough are both firmly in the hunt for automatic promotion to the Premier League and the Championship pair could be poised to enter battle away from the pitch, too, as Football League World can exclusively reveal that they are planning to rival each other for a Ligue 1 star with Lorient in a development that will interest Leeds United.

Both Birmingham and Middlesbrough have emerged among the early pace-setters in the opening months of the 2026/27 Championship campaign, which is precisely why the duo occupy the prised two automatic promotion places heading into the November international break.

Birmingham have taken significant strides following a mixed return to life in the second-tier last term, and Chris Davies' side are enjoying full-throttle form with six victories from their last seven outings to soar to the summit of the table.

Middlesbrough, meanwhile, are back with a vengeance after losing last season's play-off final in heartbreaking last-grasp fashion to Hull City, and the Teessiders appear even stronger from offsetting Hayden Hackney's big-money departure for Everton with a host of exciting additions that have helped the club to second-place at this moment in time despite mixed recent form in the injury-enforced absence of 13-goal hitman Will Lankshear.

The two Championship promotion-chasers are preparing to attack the January transfer window with extra injections of quality to help their respective efforts at getting over the line back to the Premier League once May rolls around and, in a new twist that may just interest Leeds United in the top-flight already, a fascinating battle could soon take place for a starlet out of Ligue 1.

Plans for the winter window are already heading underway in the boardrooms of Birmingham and Middlesbrough, and Football League World has learned of an exciting and highly-rated target on the radar of the second-tier's two strongest sides so far this season.

FLW can exclusively disclose that Blues and Boro are plotting moves for Lorient sensation Arthur Avom, who has emerged as one of Ligue 1's most promising young midfield talents in recent years.

Sources close to FLW have revealed that the two outfits are poised to rival each other in a bid to land Avom's sought-after signature, with the midfielder's current contract in Brittany due to expire at the end of the current campaign.

Still aged just 21, Avom would provide esteemed pedigree after already racking up close to 80 appearances for Lorient since debuting back in the 2024/25 campaign when he helped Les Merlus achieve promotion back to the top-flight.

Football League World assesses and ranks the FIFTEEN best away fanbases in the EFL right now

He's now in his third straight season as a first-team regular and has started seven of his 10 league appearances under Alexandre Dujeux this term.

Avom has also made an impression on the international stage with his native Cameroon, earning 17 caps to date and representing the Indomitable Lions at the African Cup of Nations last year.

Ligue 1 is a hotbed for elite-level talent across the European footballing landscape and, with Avom having cemented his prestigious reputation among the division's leading midfielders within his age bracket, it's no surprise that the Cameroon international is earning an ever-increasing litany of suitors ahead of the expiration of his contract.

A previous report published by Africa Foot at the end of the summer window claimed interest from numerous clubs in England and beyond, as the likes of Leeds United, Everton, Crystal Palace and Sunderland were all linked with his services alongside Club Brugge, Fenerache and fellow Ligue 1 outfit Strasbourg.

Indeed, that report indicated an asking price worth £17.1 million, which would likely prevent either Birmingham or Middlesbrough from completing a move.

However, the important detail is that months have passed without any resolution to Avom's contractual situation after Lorient exercised a one-year extension option in order to prevent losing the midfielder on a free transfer, and as he edges ever closer to the conclusion of his terms, the French outfit will need to reluctantly lower their demands.

Should they fail to, of course, there is every chance that Avom could end up leaving for nothing.

Lorient, with an esteemed history of developing and selling on talent, are only sure to know better, and it may just turn out that Birmingham and Middlesbrough are holding out for a valuation reduction that could enable either of the ambitious second-tier pair to steal a march on even bigger clubs and land Avom under the nose of various Premier League admirers.

Make no mistake about it, Avom's existing body of work and pedigree, coupled with the sort of footballing ceiling and future potential to earn the eye of some of Europe's premiere talent-developers, means that he would represent an unprecedented coup to land in the second-tier.

The list of Premier League admirers in the defensive midfielder's wake appears to be little secret, and while that's a factor capable of working against Blues or Middlesbrough, they would have the opportunity to nurture and afford instant regular minutes to the Cameroonian before potentially taking him to the top-flight once the season wraps up.

Of course, whether Avom himself would be willing to drop to second-tier football after earning his stripes at such a young age in Ligue 1 remains open to debate and will likely be determined in due course, but either way, it's a telling reflection of the immediate and longer-term ambitions in equal measure held within these two particular clubs and that should serve to excite both sets of supporters.

A dynamic ball-winning midfielder with relentless energy and the ability to both break up and initiate play forward with powerful and progressive upfield carries, Avom's skillset would seamlessly translate to any Championship engine room and has unsurprisingly caught the attention of clubs throughout the continent.

Birmingham City and Middlesbrough fans, what do you make of your club's interest in Lorient prodigy Arthur Avom? Should the boat be pushed out to complete this sort of signing in January? Let us know your thoughts in the comments below!

There's much to be done, then, if either Birmingham or Middlesbrough are to seal what could just be one of the Championship's most impressive and unprecedented acquisitions in a number of years, but stranger things have happened and both clubs have the incentive of potential Premier League football as early as next season to offer.

#2169. Robinson on the brink but Aberdeen’s deep-rooted problems go far beyond the manager

Friday marked the 40th anniversary of Alex Ferguson quitting Aberdeen for Manchester United, leaving faces like granite behind him.

Media outlets wheeled out old, grainy footage from the day of mourning, as BBC cameras conveyed the lunchtime grief of shell-shocked boozers in King Street’s Pittodrie Bar.

One old boy in a flat cap condemned the lack of loyalty from a man who demanded absolute fidelity from his players. Another proffered the view that Fergie would regret a sudden rush of blood to the head. The fan who felt the move might present Aberdeen with a bit of a problem was a master of understatement.

Four decades since they lost their Napoleon, Aberdeen are a club haunted by the past. Battlefield generals like Ferguson and Willie Miller set the bar so high, those who followed were guaranteed to shoot themselves in the feet.

Read more: Haissem Hassan Celtic absence explained as ‘manoeuvring’ costs winger

The club’s last league title came in May 1985. Major trophies have been as rare as snow in April.

While no one has any expectation of European glory these days, supporters reared on tales of the halcyon days are entitled to expect more than three finishes in the bottom six of the SPFL Premiership since 2021/22.  With a staff cost base of around £16million a year, that’s a scandalous state of affairs.

Had the Beeb returned to the Pittodrie Bar after Saturday’s 4-0 hammering from Hibs, they would have found a support happy to see the latest Aberdeen manager off the premises. Had Stephen Robinson been up for a move to Manchester, they’d have revved the car and driven him south themselves.

When the Northern Irishman leaves, it won’t be by choice. It never is these days.

Under contract until 2029, managerial pay-offs have become an expensive annual event.

And, for the Dons hierarchy, it might now be time to time ask themselves an awkward question. What if all those sacked bosses were never really the problem?

In the last five years, Derek McInnes, Stephen Glass, Jim Goodwin, Barry Robson and Jimmy Thelin all left with a black bin bag tucked under their arm. Robinson will be next to collect his P45 after a hideous second-half collapse against Hibs.

The Aberdeen job is now a poisoned chalice. Hiring managers who have done well elsewhere, the club chews them up, spits them out, then watches on as they move somewhere else and remind everyone that they were never a bad boss in the first place.

McInnes came to within minutes of a league title at Hearts last season and is now breathing down the necks of Celtic as manager of Rangers.

While Glass was always an odd, left-field choice, he had a useful two years at USL Championship side Memphis 901 and was last seen serving as Robbie Keane’s assistant at Ferencvaros.

Goodwin recovered from an ignominious Scottish Cup calamity in Darvel to prove himself a better manager than he looked the day he walked across the Easter Road pitch with a man bag over his shoulder after a 6-0 defeat to Hibs. Dundee United are now a club five places and six points better off than Aberdeen.

Cork City were promoted to the League of Ireland Premier Division last month, earning Barry Robson a new two-year contract in the process.

Two years ago this week, Jimmy Thelin had Aberdeen joint-top of the Premiership after 11 games of the 2024/25 season. Since then, the team have played 77 league games and won 19. That’s a win rate of just 25 per cent.

Supporters never really wanted Robinson in the first place. The people running the club never seemed sold on him either.

The plan was to make an overseas appointment, with Eirik Horneland the preferred candidate in March. When talks with the Norwegian stalled, Sandro Schwarz flew in to watch a game against Celtic, took one look and decided he was better off moving to Moscow.

A solid operator at Motherwell and St Mirren, Robinson was the last man standing at the bar.

Portrayed – harshly – as a long-ball, survival specialist, there was always a question over his ability to win hearts and minds at Aberdeen. To convince the critics he had to hit the ground running and, despite scratching a win over Hearts, that didn’t happen.

A thumping 4-0 win in Motherwell in September proved a false dawn when stats showed that the Dons converted all four of their shots on target and enjoyed possession of just 31 per cent.

Since Fir Park, they have won just once in seven games, against St Mirren. And the Northern Irishman’s demeanour has grown progressively more pained. Weekly monologues on the need to be better have lost any impact.

The first big decision for new chief executive Adrian Bevington, there is a grim inevitability to how this ends and wherever he turns up next, Robinson will do what so many former Aberdeen managers do by displaying a talent for winning games of football at another club. As with so many before, it won’t happen in the Granite City.

A club which sucks the life from promising careers, an Aberdeen manager is only ever two bad games from the sack. The man in the technical area works in a culture of fear, fulfilling the role of human shield for senior figures wary of sticking their head over the parapet.

Non-executive chairman Dave Cormack is a passionate fan in the boardroom. After open-heart surgery three years ago, he tried to take a step back by appointing a chief executive to run the show. Alan Burrows has now gone and every Aberdeen departure – for there are many – turns the finger of accusation turn in Cormack’s direction.

No one can accuse the chairman of failing to back his managers. The Dons have spent substantial sums building, then rebuilding, failing teams. How much they spend is less of a problem than how they spend it.

Sporting director Lutz Pfannenstiel was hired to bring strategic and continental know-how. And, at first, the German seemed keen to make himself the centre of attention.

Last season, he was parked beside interim boss Peter Leven in the dugout. These days, you could stand on the Beach Boulevard with a set of strong binoculars and struggle to find his dot on the North Sea horizon.

In Scottish football there is a new unwritten rule. Hearts and Aberdeen, it seems, can never be successful in the same season and, while the Tynecastle club have established themselves as the third force in Scottish football – Falkirk not withstanding – Aberdeen labour in the relegation play-off place.

When Robinson goes, expect the next guy to be an overseas appointment. A blameless optimist who vaguely remembers Aberdeen winning a European trophy in the days when he was knee-high to a grasshopper, and sees a club which can – and should – be so much more than the blazing bin fire it is now.

#2168. Manchester United player ratings: United failed to win despite advantage

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United and Tottenham Hotspur returned from the international break with plenty to prove after a disastrous start to the season.

United arrived with just one win, while Tottenham came to Old Trafford without a win.

Both teams had half-chances in the first half but little to show for them. Tottenham had the better chances with chances for Dominic Solanke, Connor Gallagher and Kudus, but none could take advantage of them.

Manchester United came close in the first half to score after Bryan Mbeumo raced towards the goal on a counter-attack to find Amad. He had a difficult shot, and eventually the shot was too weak to cause any damage. The first-half display was met by boos from the Old Trafford crowd at half-time and during the first half as well.

Solanke had another chance at the start of the second half but put it over the top, amid a wrestle with Lisandro Martinez in the six-yard box. It was United, however, who started the second half with promise.

Substitutions by Michael Carrick around the hour mark made the difference as United showed intent before Mbeumo got the goal off Cunha’s low cross. Luke Shaw was at the end of a scuffle with Bentancur, who was shown a red card for headbutting Shaw.

United failed to capitalize on their one-man advantage and seemed to sit on their 1-0 lead, but Solanke punished them with a header to level the game in injury time. The draw left United with just 6 points off their first six games. The result leaves United with their worst start in 40 years.

Lenne Sammens-6          Quiet afternoon for United no.1 till the goal, where he saw the ball go into the back of the net.

Leny Yoro-6       Defensively was fine but offered little upfront. Played out of position, and he showed good defensive awareness on the right back till his substitution.

Harry Maguire-5.5            Was decent in the first half but wasn’t much of an answer to Tottenham going forward in the second half

Lisandro Martinez-5      Took his eyes off the ball, resulting in conceding a late goal off Solanke.

Luke Shaw-6      Went up high on the pitch and remained a threat with his overlapping threat. Was involved in the scuffle that got Bentancur a red card for headbutting.

Kobbie Mainoo-6.5        Brilliant defensive tackle to deny Tottenham in the 68th minute. Remained composed in tight spaces against a physical Tottenham midfield.

Youri Tielemans-6          A decent performance and was solid throughout the minutes he was on the field

Bruno Fernandes-5        Another quiet and disappointing evening for the United captain after he failed to impress on a day he needed to get a result for United.

Amad-6              A positive return from injury, showed plenty of promise on the wing but failed to get past a resolute Tottenham side

Matheus Cunha-7           One of the liveliest options for United going forward. Provided an assist to Mbeumo to put United up front

Bryan Mbeumo-7.5        A brilliant finish off Matheus Cunha’s low ball to put United in front. Was dangerous going forward and denied a second on a counter by Kinsky off a brilliant long ball by Baleba.

Carlos Baleba (58’)-6.5  A decent debut overall for the Cameroon international. He put in a couple of dangerous balls up the field, including a clear ball to Mbeumo on a counter, who was denied by Kinsky.

Diogo Dalot (58’)-6      Provided a decent attacking threat when brought on around the hour mark.

Joshua Zirkzee (58’)-6   Showed plenty of promise and was a threat after he was brought in around the hour mark.

#2167. Why Martin O'Neill has made a Kasper Hogh and Henrik Larsson Celtic comparison

Martin O'Neill has compared Kasper Hogh's situation at Celtic to that of Henrik Larsson.

Hogh has not scored for his new club since a hat-trick against Kilmarnock and has been in and out of the team after some flat performances.

After drawing a blank against Killlie this time, while having a goal disallowed, O'Neill feels the crowd are right behind him just as they were a certain fellow Scandinavian who went on to become a club legend.

The Celtic manager told BBC Radio Scotland: “I didn’t want to bring this into the melting pot.

“But a few years ago Henrik Larsson stepped onto the field here and couldn’t do anything wrong (in the eyes of the supporters).

“And that’s a lovely feeling to have, regardless of the number of times he might have misplaced a pass – which wasn’t that often.

Celtic have something special brewing with Mika Baur The turning point that inspired Celtic to big European victory against Besiktas

“The crowd were very forgiving of a fantastic footballer.

“Now I’m not saying Kasper has reached that height. But I think the crowd appreciate what he’s doing and that he’s trying to help the team.”

The 74-year-old felt that if Hogh's goal had been allowed to stand, he would have gone on to get another.

"We thought Kasper had got that goal and then it was chalked off," he added.

“Then a few minutes later he had another chance. And I think if the first ‘goal’ had counted then I think perhaps he then gets a second goal.

“It’s maybe a lack of confidence in front of goal at this minute.

“But I thought he worked exceptionally hard for us during the game – as he did on Thursday night. The goals will come.

“I think it’s a great thing when a player steps onto the pitch and think that the crowd is with them.

“That gives you a boost of confidence for a start – and to know that you can maybe make a couple of mistakes and might be forgiven.

#2166. Michael Carrick reacts to Man Utd boos after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick admitted Manchester United’s 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur was a painful result, after his side failed to protect the lead against 10-man Tottenham at Old Trafford.

Manchester United looked set to claim three points when Bryan Mbeumo scored in the second half off Cunha’s low cross, but Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser in injury time left Carrick facing more questions on United’s performance and his future.

Speaking to BBC Match of the Day, the United manager felt his players improved after the break but failed to handle the situation when victory was within reach.

“It was a mixed game. We found it tough to start with. For a number of reasons, we were not quite there. You could see it was a challenging time,” Carrick said.

“But the lads grew into the game, in the second half I thought we were better in everything. To get the game as we wanted it, in such a good position – we should see it through. We have to manage those situations.”

United have now conceded two late goals that have cost them valuable points, something Carrick believes has made their position look worse than it might have otherwise.

Asked whether a lack of confidence had contributed to United’s slow start, he disagreed but admitted matches can quickly slip away from his side.

The boos at half-time and full-time also clearly affected him.

“It’s not nice, it hurts a lot because I care about this club. But I understand the disappointment, the reality is what it is, we need to get back to winning games.”

#2165. The Birmingham City, Tom Wagner & Jay Stansfield trend that is now unmissable

In the 2023-24 campaign, Birmingham City found themselves in the top six and the play-off places when they decided to part company with John Eustace heading into the October international break.

Star of the show for The Blues in those opening couple of months had been young attacker Jay Stansfield on loan from Fulham with Stansfield building on an impressive campaign in League One at Exeter City the year before.

Stansfield’s story is well known and an emotional one, having represented Exeter and given the then retired ‘number nine’ shirt over a decade after his father, Adam, had worn that shirt for The Grecians before passing away.

In the second city at Birmingham, and still only 20 when he arrived, Stansfield had notched five goals in his first 13 starts in the second-tier but after Eustace’s surprise departure, Birmingham fell away from being early promotion challengers, eventually being relegated at the end of the campaign.

Despite that, Stansfield had continued to catch the eye with some memorable strikes in his 12-goal tally, with two assists, in the Championship – as well as a match-winning performance with a goal and an assist against Hull City in an FA Cup third round replay that season.

Such was the form shown by Stansfield, he had begun to become sought-after by many but, with Birmingham beginning a new era, it would have taken something special to bring him to League One.

Given that Chris Davies was newly-appointed and with a record-breaking summer spending spree, Stansfield was the cherry on top as he arrived from Fulham for a club-record fee of £15 million – driven by owner and chairman Tom Wagner.

Stansfield remained essential for Birmingham as Blues broke the all-time EFL points tally in the 2024-25 League One campaign with Stansfield their top scorer, hitting 19 in the league and 23 across all competitions.

Even last season, back in the Championship and only 22 having helped England’s U21 side defend the UEFA U21 European Championship over the summer, Stansfield hit double figures in the 2025-26 campaign and there would be reason to believe, with those numbers, Birmingham have landed themselves a future legend with over 50 goals for the club already and still as such a young age.

There would have to be some concerns, though, that the unreasonable expectation placed upon him due to his fee has contributed to periods of poor form or potentially low confidence.

For example, for a player that smashed the all-time transfer record for League One and was the spearhead of the most dominant team ever at that level; Stansfield’s initial goals tally of 19 seems reasonable. Once delved into further and seeing that nine of those 19 were from the penalty spot.

Ten open play goals for a team as dominant as that Birmingham and for the fee shelled out; it wouldn’t be unreasonable to suggest the pressure was on Stansfield, perhaps more from outsiders than City supporters, to kick on and prove himself more emphatically in the Championship last season.

He eventually did scrape himself into double figures with a goal in Birmingham’s penultimate home game of the campaign but that, again, is a fairly underwhelming return after hitting eight in their first 16 games of the season.

What is a trend that has occurred would be that Stansfield appears to be at his best when chairman and owner, and the architect of his big money move, Tom Wagner is in attendance at St Andrew’s.

Wagner, who continues to fully recover from a stroke that he suffered in February but is said to now be in better shape, was in attendance at the weekend as Birmingham pulled off an impressive and potentially pivotal 2-1 defeat of Swansea City with Stansfield notching a brace.

That made it three goals in two games for Stansfield after had scored their third in a 3-0 defeat of Millwall a few days’ earlier – having previously managed just one strike in his first 13 appearances so far this season.

A theme of all of those matches in which Stansfield had scored in was that Wagner was in attendance, with the first goal back in September coming in a 2-2 draw against Wolverhampton Wanderers before those goals in successive matches in the past week.

Wagner, as he continues to recover, is unable to attend every match but a theme is now repeating that when he does, it tends to lead to Stansfield getting himself on the scoresheet.

Correlation does not mean causation, obviously, but there is reason to believe or at least acknowledge that Stansfield, should he indeed be someone to struggle with some confidence issues as any footballer does from time to time, does feel that confidence when Wagner is in attendance.

In fact, Stansfield himself has previously discussed the impact of having Wagner in the stands, doing so after the American returned for his first game back at St Andrew’s since suffering that stroke in February by going to Blues’ 2-2 draw with Wolves:

Stansfield said: “Tom is a special person so for him to be here and knowing he’s recovering well is really positive.

“He gave the stadium a massive lift. That’s what Tom’s character is, he puts smiles on people’s faces. As a team, for us to walk out and see him there and for the crowd to be signing his name special for him as well.

“He’s been in contact a lot more in the last few weeks which is good, giving me confidence, trying to push the team, saying everything is going to be good and things.

“He kept saying ‘I’ll see you soon’ so I was waiting for the time to be able to go and see him. I’m really looking forward to going to see him and making sure he’s alright and having a good conversation.”

Stansfield has even gone as far as to describe Wagner as a ‘father figure’ more recently than that and so him once again finding his best with Wagner in attendance is no real surprise:

Marko Stamenic's red card for the visitors at St. Andrew's has caused plenty of online debate

The former Fulham man outlined: “When I first joined here he had faith in me, he backed me all the way, and even when times got tough during League One he always messaged me saying 'stay confident, do this, do that.'

“I got a message from him before the Wolves game saying 'Just go out and be yourself, I'm sure you'll score' and that happened so it was a great feeling and a great day.

“I can't thank him enough for the opportunity that he gave me to come back to this football club. The first season we were in the Championship we built a good relationship, he liked the way I pressed, got around the pitch and also showed my quality.

“For him to have faith in me and bring me back at a crucial time in my career... I was looking at it and I needed first team football week in, week out and a lot of people asked questions why I moved back to Birmingham and it's probably a decision I'll never look back on. It's changed my life and also my family's life as well.

“He's a father figure and even the little messages that he sends, you read them and you think let's go out and do it.”

So, while correlation does not mean causation, there is probably just cause to believe the trend and pattern of Stansfield’s performance and confidence improving in the presence of Wagner is real.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

#2164. Bryan Mbeumo reacts to Man Utd draw with Tottenham

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bryan Mbeumo admitted Manchester United have work to do after their frustrating 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, despite scoring a superb second-half goal at Old Trafford.

The Cameroon international put Manchester United ahead in the 73rd minute, finishing in the far corner after Matheus Cunha created the opportunity from a low cross. However, Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory, even though Tottenham had been reduced to 10 men following Rodrigo Bentancur’s red card.

Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2).

3 – Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2). Disadvantage? pic.twitter.com/DuSpvymZfu

Speaking to Sky Sports after the game, Mbeumo acknowledged that United had taken too long to find their rhythm.

“We took time to get into the game, but we played well in the second half. In the first half, that wasn’t us, we had to change. We tried to get at them,” he said.

Mbeumo recognised the threat Tottenham posed and admitted the result was disappointing, particularly given United’s position in the game before Solanke’s late goal.

“We know Spurs have good players who can be dangerous. It’s a bit disappointing, we need to work on things.”

United have struggled for consistency in the opening weeks of the season, and dropping points after taking the lead will do little to ease the pressure on Carrick. Mbeumo, however, insisted the team must look ahead rather than dwell on the setback.

“We need to keep going forward, as we have a lot of games.”

Michael Carrick was pleased with Mbeumo’s contribution, highlighting both the quality of his finish and the different roles he has played since joining United.

“Bryan’s done great. It was a fantastic finish again; he’s obviously played different roles for us but we know what he offers,” the United manager said.

“He’s got good quality and obviously the more goals he scores, the better. He’s doing really well for us.”

Mbeumo’s goal was one of the few bright moments in another difficult afternoon for United. Carrick’s team must now find a way to turn things around from here after just six points from six games.

#2163. Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp tear into Man Utd after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have come under fresh criticism after their disappointing 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, with Sky Sports pundits Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp questioning the team’s performance and the challenges facing Michael Carrick.

Manchester United looked on course for a much-needed victory at Old Trafford after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half. However, despite Tottenham being reduced to 10 men, Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser denied Carrick’s side all three points.

Gary Neville felt Tottenham deserved the points, pointing to Solanke’s late goal and his impact as Tottenham captain.

“Tottenham deserved a point. That was a really important moment for Solanke. He’d missed some big chances and was captain for the first time,” Neville said on Sky Sports.

“They were the better team by far. If they’d left without a point, it would have been terrible for them.”

The former United defender also expressed concern about what the result means for Carrick, who faces increasing pressure to improve performances and results.

“Michael Carrick is left with a lot to think about. It was a worrying day in terms of the level of performance and a big week ahead.”

Manchester United spent massively in the summer to overhaul their midfield. However, Jamie Redknapp was critical of the performance he saw on the pitch.

“I do not see enough connections in that midfield, and they look devoid of any confidence, ideas, or patterns of play, playing out the back,” Redknapp said.

“Tottenham were just much better than them in every department, but that is a theme this season.”

The former Liverpool midfielder also questioned United’s energy and attacking organization, suggesting the squad is not strong enough to meet the demands placed upon it.

“Their energy levels are not good enough, and they look loose going forward; there was no real structure.”

Redknapp added that Carrick cannot fix every issue alone, insisting the manager needs backing to improve the squad.

“The squad is not good or strong enough, and Michael [Carrick] needs help in that respect.”
