#2162. ‘Embarrassing themselves!’ Jamie Carragher hits back at Rio Ferdinand over Cristiano Ronaldo defence

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Carragher has taken aim at Rio Ferdinand for defending Cristiano Ronaldo following the Portuguese forward’s latest controversy with his national team.

Ronaldo has found himself at the centre of a growing dispute after abruptly leaving Portugal’s national team camp following a reported disagreement with head coach Jorge Jesus. The incident has prompted strong reactions from former teammates and pundits, with Ferdinand firmly siding with the Al-Nassr star.

Jamie Carragher, however, believes Ronaldo’s reputation should not shield him from criticism when his behaviour becomes difficult to defend.

Rio Ferdinand, who played alongside Cristiano Ronaldo at Manchester United between 2003 and 2009, criticised Jorge Jesus for the way he handled the situation.

Speaking on his Rio Presents podcast, the former United captain criticised Jesus for not showing respect to Cristiano Ronaldo.

“Cristiano Ronaldo was put in a very bad situation and was treated in an inappropriate way. He has an enormous history in football. Jorge Jesus did not deal with Ronaldo well and chose to treat him in that way, but I cannot find any explanation for it,” Ferdinand said.

Despite Jesus being Portugal’s manager, Ferdinand questioned his standing, comparing him with the likes of Sir Alex Ferguson and Pep Guardiola. To say the least, Ferdinand only embarrassed himself doing that.

Jamie Carragher saw the argument very differently and rightfully so.

Speaking on The Overlap Daily, the former Liverpool defender suggested that Ferdinand’s friendship with Ronaldo was influencing his reaction to the incident.

“People think it’s good to be close to people in the game, but this is where it becomes a problem,” Carragher explained.

He then called out both Ferdinand and broadcaster Piers Morgan, who has also been vocal in his support of Ronaldo.

“People like Piers Morgan and Rio Ferdinand, who are big people in the media whether you like them or not, are embarrassing themselves with the nonsense that they’re coming out with because they know Ronaldo. You can know someone and try to look after them, but you cannot defend the indefensible.”

Carragher believes Ronaldo’s achievements should not put the forward beyond criticism, particularly when his actions involve his national team and its management.

Gary Neville, who also played with Cristiano Ronaldo, has questioned Ronaldo’s handling of the situation, despite his longstanding affection for his former Manchester United teammate.

Neville argued that Ronaldo’s public response had made matters worse and the forward needed to accept criticism rather than expect others to defend him.

“With Cristiano, it’s difficult because I love him, but I can’t watch what happened at United in his second period and then see him make a statement like that without saying something. He has to be pulled up on that and told that it isn’t acceptable,” Neville said.

He also suggested Ronaldo’s extraordinary success, wealth and influence could have contributed to a sense that he was beyond challenge.

“When you have a billion pounds in the bank, the achievements he has had and the power he has, you feel you’re unbreakable, you feel you’re unstoppable,” Neville added.

Despite his criticism, Neville does not believe the episode will define Ronaldo’s legacy permanently. He expects the attention to fade once the forward retires, allowing his achievements on the pitch to take centre stage again.

#2161. EFL League One's top 9 toughest fanbases named and ranked by AI

The League One lineup of clubs this season is, on paper, one of the strongest it has ever been, with nine former Premier League sides and a few more who have spent time in the top flight previously, too.

With that, there are a lot of big, tough fanbases who have seen their sides reach the highest heights in English football, and have followed them through the more frequent lows that the sport has served them up in recent years.

But there are also some, quote, unquote, "smaller" fanbases who pack out both their own stadiums and away ends to create a tough, hostile environment. That's what this list focuses on.

Football League World has asked the AI-based service ChatGPT to rank the toughest fanbases in the third tier this season. When deciding, AI took into account intimidation, numbers, noise and the fanbases' reputation. Here are the top nine.

Wigan Athletic's fanbase is often looked down upon by certain critics given the history of rugby league in the town and the fact that they play in a ground which holds over 25,000, yet their average attendance hovers around the 10,000 mark.

However, when comparing that to other clubs in the division, their average attendance is near the top half, and it's a loud, passionate fanbase that follows the club well up and down the country.

We rank all 24 League One stadiums by their ground's distance from the city or town, starting with the longest distance.

AI also made sure to reference all the turbulent times that have surrounded the club in recent years, through numerous ownership groups and administrations. They're still vocal in support of their side, and it creates quite an underrated, gritty fanbase in League One.

Another club who have gone through tough times in the past decade, but still have a loyal fanbase who stick by them are Blackpool.

ChatGPT outlines the Tangerine Army as a 'proud fanbase' with a strong identity, and with their recognisable branding and colour scheme, it's not wrong.

When Bloomfield Road gets going, there aren't many more intimidating grounds in the EFL. Last season, Blackpool's survival could almost be attributed to their decision to slash ticket prices, giving fans the chance to buy a five-game bundle for £65 and pack the stadium ahead of the final push. They won all five.

This season has been difficult once again under Ian Evatt, and you feel that this tough fanbase will have to stick with their team again to try and avoid a similar scare to last year.

Barnsley are arguably the fanbase in League One going through the most right now regarding their ownership. The ire towards Neerav Parekh is as loud as it ever has been, with the Tykes regressing year by year and now staring down the barrel of a potential relegation.

There have been murmurs of protests during games, as a young, inexperienced squad does its best under the leadership of a returning Daniel Stendel. Whether that happens or not as the season goes on is to be seen.

Nevertheless, when Oakwell is full, which hasn't been the case for several years now, the loud South Yorkshire fanbase can really make a difference. AI hasn't forgotten that, hence Barnsley's place on this list.

There aren't many more difficult places to come in the EFL than the Kenny on a Tuesday night — or any night, for that matter.

The tight ground at Kenilworth Road creates a feeling of the fanbase being right on top of you, and even last season, with there being discontent at Luton Town amongst the fanbase due to their consecutive relegations, home form was at play-off level; it was away from home which let them down.

Five players, five careers. Name them from the clubs they played for.

Each day there are five current or former EFL players to identify. For each one you’ll see their senior clubs in the order they played for them. Loan spells are left out.

You get four guesses per player. Every wrong guess unlocks a clue: first their position, then their nationality, then their initials.

Get it first time for 4 points, then 3, 2 and 1. The best possible score is 20. A new set of players arrives at midnight.

That's arguably why the Hatters don't rank closer towards the podium on this list, but their recent experiences in the top flight and in Wembley finals create quite the reputable, 'tough' fanbase to beat.

There'll be hopes that it doesn't fade away once they move out of their famous home and into the stadium at Power Court in 2028.

"A historically established football town" seems quite an understatement from ChatGPT when describing Huddersfield Town. The Terriers were the best team in England in the infancy of the Football League, and that's created a fanbase which has passed down through generations.

Like some of the other clubs on the list, Huddersfield have had those Premier League years recently before sinking into League One, and while there's still room to grow the attendance average at this moment in time, it's still a loud atmosphere at the Accu Stadium.

It's a fanbase that doesn't accept mediocrity, especially at this level, which can sometimes create a negative feeling around the club, but when things are going well, there aren't many in the division who are better.

It must be incredibly tough for Plymouth Argyle fans to travel quite literally up and down the country every other Saturday for away games, and that alone is enough to see them on this list, per ChatGPT.

Even their closest away day in the league this season takes them to Reading, which is over three hours away in the car. It's quite some effort, and the fact that their following on the road is better than most is quite frankly ridiculous.

The Green Army is incredibly intimidating at Home Park, too, and while form at home hasn't been as strong under Tom Cleverley as it has in recent years, there was a time under Steven Schumacher in the Championship where all they did was win at home.

They're one of the more reputable fanbases in England, and well worthy of a place on this list.

Not all fierce, tough fanbases have been sharpened by a fall from grace; others build their own from the ashes of what they used to be. Stockport County epitomises that.

Even back during their time in the EFL in the 2000s, before they fell all the way to the National League North, Edgeley Park wasn't nearly as full as it is now, and the plans to expand the stadium would have been laughed at.

Whether it's due to fans of the big Manchester clubs deciding to follow a local club on the rise due to being disconnected from the top level, or just the success breeding new fans, this is a passionate supporter base that makes their ground a very difficult place to come.

AI states that "Valley Parade is a seriously difficult away day." Once again, a rather simple analogy. Up until their recent defeat in the West Yorkshire Derby against Huddersfield, it was a fortress that, if you did somehow come away with anything, it'd be by some stroke of luck.

Since December 2024, Bradford City have won 31 league games at home, dropping points just 14 times. That's a seriously good record, and it's almost all down to the sheer noise that the Bantams fans create in the ground.

Their standing as a big-city club helps build a passionate set of supporters, who fill away ends, too, and if they end up climbing back towards the Premier League, they'd more than deserve it.

But there was always going to be one side topping this list. In terms of fanbase size and their recent troubled past, Sheffield Wednesday have a really tough, hardened set of fans.

The fact they were able to stay away from Hillsborough during the final days of the Dejphon Chansiri reign in protest, losing out on what is, for some, a real escape from day-to-day struggles, was incredibly commendable, and it was their support which has helped the club get back on its feet under David Storch.

Who do you think has the toughest fanbase in League One? Let us know in the comments.

Now, Hillsborough is rocking again, with 30,000 home fans each game, creating quite an intimidating atmosphere that some clubs have crumbled in already this season.

Their home record does need to improve, though, when an away side does get up for the occasion. New boss Dave Challinor will have a lot of demanding fans to try and satisfy in his new role.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

#2159. Is Mainoo-Tielemans the right midfield option for Man Utd?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United drew 1-1 at home to Tottenham despite having an extra man advantage. The Reds saw Mbeumo put them in front, but Solanke scored a late leveller in injury time after Bentancur was shown a red card for headbutting Luke Shaw.

Manchester United’s worrisome season continues after accumulating just six points off the first six games, their first start in 40 years. But more than the results, what worries Manchester United is their midfield pairing.

United spent massively on their midfield rebuild and somehow can’t find the best combination in the midfield. Michael Carrick has gone with the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership, but they have failed to control the midfield. Individually, they both come with decent numbers, but collectively they fall short.

Mainoo and Tielemans struggled against Tottenham while closing the midfield and at times showed a lack of energy. On the day when nothing clicked for United, the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership particularly raised eyebrows.

Gary Neville has also raised serious concerns about Manchester United’s Mainoo-Tielemans partnership.

Neville was particularly unimpressed by the midfield balance, arguing that the problems went beyond one disappointing performance.

The former United captain stopped short of ruling out the combination altogether, but he suggested he has serious doubts about its suitability.

“Tielemans and Mainoo, I’m not calling time on it, that’s probably too drastic, but I’ve seen enough of it to think that’s not a midfield that’s dynamic enough to play at this football club in the Premier League.”

Former Spurs midfielder Redknapp felt United struggled to connect their midfield with the rest of the team during the opening stages.

He added: “I didn’t see enough connections in that midfield in the first hour. Baleba came on and did OK and showed a bit more.

“They look devoid of any confidence, any idea, any patterns of play, playing out from the back. Tottenham were much better than them in every single department.”

While Carlos Baleba was a particular highlight on the day, he showed great potential when brought on. He brought a new sense of energy in the midfield and sent a few balls over the top for a couple of quick counterattacks as well.

Redknapp also pointed to the physical demands of modern Premier League football, arguing that United cannot afford to be outworked by their opponents.

Tottenham ran a staggering 5 km more than Manchester United, something Redknapp felt was a pattern.

"Spurs ran 5km more than Man United… it's like that EVERY GAME!"Jamie Redknapp is less than impressed with the lack of ground covered this season by Manchester United 😬 pic.twitter.com/BxWkFuyGJ1

“There is a pattern that I don’t really like when I watch teams, and that’s when every time the stats don’t lie and the other team runs more than you.

“I feel in Premier League football right now there are a couple of non-negotiables, and one is you have to run more than the other team if you want to win or at least get close enough. Tonight Spurs ran 5km more than Manchester United.”

He believes Tottenham’s pressing exposed the lack of pace in United’s midfield, making it difficult for the hosts to move the ball safely through the centre.

“In terms of how they played today, I think they have an issue with Tielemans and Mainoo in midfield. I don’t see enough energy in there. Baleba will add a lot and will give them a tremendous amount of energy but right now, when things aren’t going well… and Tottenham knew it!

“Every time United played the ball out from the back they pounced. They pressed them because they knew United don’t have the pace and speed in midfield to get away.”

“In isolation, one of them is absolutely fine, the two of them together doesn’t work for me. They haven’t got the pace or the energy to get around the pitch for 90 minutes.”

#2158. Melvine Malard becomes Chelsea’s leading scorer as FOUR-goal start strengthens her claim to the No.9 role

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Melvine Malard’s Chelsea goals are beginning to provide a persuasive answer to the club’s centre-forward question.

Her opener in Sunday’s 3-0 victory over West Ham took the summer signing to four competitive goals, making her Chelsea’s leading scorer this season.

That total comprises two goals in Champions League qualifying and two in the Women’s Super League.

The circumstances make those contributions particularly useful. Across three scoring appearances, Malard has broken a deadlock, restored a lead and helped Chelsea put difficult matches beyond their opponents.

Three of those goals arrived while Chelsea were level or protecting a one-goal advantage.

That gives the early return significance beyond its size. Malard has repeatedly contributed while there was still a contest to resolve.

Sunday provided the latest example. Chelsea had plenty of possession but needed somebody to turn an opening into a lead.

Turn and finish 💥Melvine Malard finishes for @ChelseaFCW 🔵#BarclaysWSL pic.twitter.com/eicBNrYMYk

According to Chelsea’s official match report, the Blues reached the half-hour mark without a shot on target despite having 64 per cent possession.

Sjoeke Nüsken created the breakthrough by winning possession and finding Malard.

The striker received the ball with her back to goal, held her position, turned and finished into the bottom corner.

It was an important demonstration of what she can offer centrally. Chelsea could find her under pressure and still get a shot away, rather than needing to release her into open space.

Nüsken’s contribution deserves equal recognition. Her pressing created the opportunity that Malard converted.

The sequence offers Bompastor something useful to build on: winning possession higher up the pitch and finding a forward capable of finishing before the opposition recover.

Malard’s first three Chelsea goals came as a substitute.

Her two-goal competitive debut against Real Sociedad immediately demonstrated that capacity to change a match.

Nüsken released her for the goal that restored Chelsea’s lead at 3-2. Ellie Carpenter subsequently supplied her second, extending the advantage to three goals.

The official account of that European victory records two different routes to goal: a run beyond the defence and a finish from a delivery across the box.

Against Birmingham, her alertness produced another valuable contribution. Malard punished hesitation over a backpass to score Chelsea’s second, eight minutes after coming on, as detailed in the club’s match report.

Those appearances give Bompastor flexibility. Malard has already influenced matches when introduced against tiring opponents, while West Ham supplied a scoring return from the starting role.

Chelsea signed Malard from Manchester United on a four-year contract, bringing her back together with a coach who first identified her potential at 14.

Bompastor later coached her in Lyon’s senior side. Their relationship therefore predates this summer’s recruitment by years.

Speaking when Chelsea announced her signing, Malard said:

“My objective is to become better every single day and to win trophies.”

Her next task is sustaining this contribution across a longer run of matches.

Competition remains productive. Aggie Beever-Jones replaced her at West Ham and supplied Maika Hamano’s late goal, giving Bompastor another reason to value both forwards.

Malard’s strongest argument is the range of situations she has already influenced. She has attacked space, finished crosses, punished hesitation and scored with a defender close behind her.

Four goals offer an encouraging early return. The variety behind them gives Chelsea something substantial to develop.

#2156. One lesson Senne Lammens learned after Tottenham’s late equaliser

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Senne Lammens believes Manchester United must learn to finish games off after their late collapse against Tottenham Hotspur.

The result left the goalkeeper frustrated at Old Trafford despite leading the game and with a player more on the field.

Manchester United looked set for three points after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half, but Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory.

Speaking to MUTV, Senne Lammens admitted the team had struggled early on but had done enough after the break to put themselves in a winning position.

“Football can be a bit cruel at times,” the Belgian goalkeeper said. “There are a lot of ups and downs, especially in a game like that.”

Senne Lammens felt United failed to take advantage of Tottenham’s red card, allowing the visitors too much space to find a late equaliser.

“We’ve won a lot of games against 10 men [in the past], but you also know there can be a tricky situation when you’re playing against 10 men, because they have nothing to lose and they know that.”

He added that United should have scored again to put the result beyond doubt or, at the very least, prevented Tottenham from creating further chances.

“I think that’s what we did wrong. We gave them a little bit too much breathing room to create those chances, and then one ball has to go right for them, one bounce has to go their way, and they can score like that.”

The goalkeeper also pointed to similarities with United’s earlier disappointment against Everton, where points slipped away late in the match.

“You feel like those games just slip away from you, and there are one or two situations where you don’t really have control,” he said.

“I think there’s a lot to learn from today. It wasn’t all negative, not at all, but especially the way it ended left a very bad feeling.”

“I think the easy answer is that we have to be able to kill the game faster and not put ourselves in that position to give them a chance.”

Despite the frustration, Lammens said there were positives to take from the performance. However, the late goal had left a bitter feeling, and United now need to turn those lessons into better results.

#2155. Danny Drinkwater on Chelsea move he ‘could never turn down’

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Danny Drinkwater Chelsea story still carries a sting. Five years at Stamford Bridge gave him plenty of time to think about how it all turned out, and his own verdict is not a happy one.

Drinkwater opened up in a Truth or Padel appearance alongside David Ornstein. He said the move was one he could never have turned down, even with the benefit of hindsight. “No, I don’t regret moving. I regret, in hindsight, how it’s turned out. I think I could never turn it down,” he said.

Strip away the headlines and the numbers alone tell an uncomfortable story. Drinkwater spent five years on Chelsea’s books. In that time he made just 23 appearances, according to the original interview report.

Do the maths and it barely comes to five games a year. That is a thin return for a central midfielder signed to compete for a first-team place. It is also the kind of record that rarely sits well at a club of Chelsea’s size and ambition.

Chelsea’s best attacking spells over a similar stretch of seasons look far more productive, as our striker rankings show. Set next to those numbers, Drinkwater’s five quiet years stand out even more starkly.

Five years. Twenty three games. These numbers alone explains why this transfer still gets talked about by Drinkwater, long after the headlines faded.

The numbers only tell part of the story. A string of incidents turned a quiet, underwhelming spell into something far more pointed.

Drinkwater had a training-ground bust-up while out on loan. He was also involved in an on-pitch brawl with Chelsea’s own under-23s side. Flashpoints between a first-team player and a development squad are rare at any club, let alone one with Chelsea’s standards.

Taken alone, either incident might have passed as a bad day. Taken together, it became harder to see Drinkwater simply as an unlucky squad player who could not catch a break.

The most serious moment came during his time at Aston Villa. Drinkwater headbutted team-mate Jota and was fined two weeks’ wages for it. He says the consequences at Chelsea did not stop there.

“They didn’t formally ban me, but I got removed from the first team building after the incident at Villa,” Drinkwater said.

One off-field incident, at another club, pushed him out of the manager’s day-to-day plans back home. No formal punishment at Chelsea followed. The effect was much the same.

Chelsea fans saw a similar breakdown of trust play out when Graham Potter struggled to win over the dressing room during another difficult Chelsea transition. Trust can break down fast once results or behaviour turn against the person at the centre of it.

Drinkwater’s comments matter beyond his own career. They are a reminder that saying yes to a big move does not guarantee it will work once a player walks through the door.

That tension has not gone away. Chelsea’s current transfer business, covered in our transfer watch, shows the club still weighing big calls on players whose futures depend on more than form alone.

Squad management under Xabi Alonso, assessed in our bomb squad verdict, raises a familiar issue. Players who fall outside a manager’s plans rarely find a quick way back in, whether the exile is formal or not.

Drinkwater never found his way back into Chelsea’s plans after the Villa incident. His account of the Danny Drinkwater Chelsea years stands as a blunt warning. A move that looks impossible to refuse can still become one of the hardest spells of a career.

#2154. Coventry & Stoke fans notice 'disrespectful' thing Mark Robins has been spotted doing

Stoke City are flying in the EFL Championship now, with Mark Robins' side sitting third in the table heading into the November international break.

The Potters started the season with three straight defeats, but have lost just once in the Championship since August.

A 2-1 win over Portsmouth at the weekend was their third win on the spin, following victories against Preston North End and Blackburn Rovers.

Stoke's win at Portsmouth was a big one, particularly with Robins' side falling behind in only the sixth minute of the game to Tyrese Shade's goal.

Jack McGlynn equalised just before the hour, with the turnaround complete on 77 minutes after Ben Wilmot's goal.

Footage emerged of Robins showing his thanks to the travelling Stoke fans at Fratton Park post-match, with the 56-year-old giving some celebratory fist pumps towards the away end, before reminding the camera it's now five wins on the spin on the road - the first time the Potters have achieved that since 1947.

The controversy around the fist pumps comes from the Coventry City fans, with @JohnSmithtkdy pointing out that during his spells in-charge of the Sky Blues - the latest of which was 2017-2024 where he kick-started the Sky Blues' rise back through the EFL - he wouldn't celebrate like that.

@LeeDeeTicks backed up the statement, stating how Frank Lampard started that tradition straight away with the Sky Blues: "A bit harsh but I hear it. Super Frank did it on his first game with us 🩵"

@mrtjnss also responded, albeit with a touch or irony in their tone: "Yeah personally i cant believe it. Totally disrespectful. Devastated."

The Stoke fans also had something to say, including @ShaneeScFc, who wanted to point out how Coventry fans might seem 'plastic': "Cause he’s infront of a proper fan base and not a set of plastics who decided to turn up 5 years ago 👀"

@scfcprims pointed out how good the Stoke fans have been: "Because our fans deserved it. Packed out every away end and unbelievable support."

There were some pretty simple messages, too.

Communal singing is a big part of the matchday experience, so FLW have asked AI to evaluate which clubs have the best anthems in the EFL.

Without sounding too much like Roy Keane, celebrations in football have changed.

There was a time when managers wouldn't dream of fist pumping to the fans after a win (it's their job), but it's also now a big part of the game and managers up and down the country, whatever the level, do this kind of thing.

A fair few managers are still an exception to the rule, and before seeing this footage, you'd have thought Robins would be one of those. It doesn't really seem his style to be geeing up the crowd after a win, and in fairness here, it's quite a tame attempt.

That said, as he points out, his side have just matched a record that's stood since 1947, and the fans have made a long trip to Portsmouth. They deserve seeing their manager enjoying a win - just as Coventry do every time Lampard is celebrating three points in the Premier League.

#2153. Keira Walsh adds another Chelsea weapon as former Arsenal target scores THIRD goal of the season

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Keira Walsh is giving Chelsea opponents another decision to make: protect the penalty area or step out to stop her shooting.

Her goal in Sunday’s 3-0 victory at West Ham took her to three for the season across all competitions. Each has come from outside the box.

For a player whose passing helps organise Chelsea’s attacks, that threat adds another way to influence matches. Recent performances show her contributing both to the construction of chances and their finish.

Against Sociedad, Walsh struck from distance after a corner, helping Chelsea complete their passage into the Champions League’s league phase.

The club’s account of that qualifying victory describes a tight encounter in which clear opportunities were limited.

At Manchester United, Lucy Bronze found her near the edge of the box and Walsh drove her finish into the bottom corner.

She also supplied Lauren James’ goal that afternoon, as Chelsea’s match report records. It was a useful illustration of the choices she offers: attempt the finish herself or release somebody closer to goal.

West Ham provided another example of that balance.

Walsh had already struck the post before Alyssa Thompson carried the ball in from the left and found Wieke Kaptein. Kaptein moved it on to Walsh, whose finish gave Chelsea breathing space.

The official report also records an earlier Walsh run down the channel and delivery towards Sjoeke Nüsken.

Her involvement therefore extended beyond waiting for opportunities to shoot. She helped Chelsea approach the penalty area from different positions.

There was a similar reward against Arsenal a week earlier. Walsh’s saved effort fell to Thompson for the only goal, as covered in our report of Chelsea’s derby victory.

That sequence illustrates why a dangerous shot can matter even when the goalkeeper stops it. Chelsea had a runner ready to attack the rebound.

Our assessment is that Walsh’s shooting gives defenders an additional problem. Closing her down can open a passing route; allowing her time leaves the goalkeeper exposed to a player already finding her range.

Walsh has also explained why she feels comfortable alongside Potter.

Speaking after the Arsenal victory in an interview published by Chelsea, she said:

“Lexi is really intelligent. She makes me feel really comfortable when I’m playing next to her.”

Her wider assessment emphasised collective responsibility, with team-mates covering for each other and working to make Chelsea harder to beat.

That matters when assessing midfield influence. Walsh’s freedom to help an attack depends partly on the positioning and decisions of those around her.

Potter subsequently started alongside her in Chelsea’s confirmed team against Lyon, giving their partnership another demanding European assignment.

The encouraging feature is the trust Walsh describes. Bompastor has an experienced organiser working with a younger midfielder whose reading of the game she clearly values.

BBC Sport reported that Barcelona rejected Arsenal’s bid for Walsh in September 2024.

Chelsea completed her signing in January 2025 on a four-and-a-half-year contract. Her official club profile outlines the technical quality and ability to dictate tempo that underpinned that recruitment.

Those qualities remain central to her value. Three early goals strengthen the attacking contribution around them, without establishing that she has changed position or should abandon her deeper responsibilities.

For Bompastor, the opportunity is to preserve that balance: Walsh helping Chelsea progress through midfield, supporting those around her and recognising when the space opens for a finish.

#2152. Manchester United defence: Why Carrick must trust Yoro and Heaven

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United endured another embarrassing draw against 10-man Tottenham after failing to register their second win despite bringing in a plethora of players.

Manchester United invested heavily last season in their attacking options, bringing in Bryan Mbeumo and Matheus Cunha.

This season, they overhauled their midfield. United spent massively on Carlos Baleba, Youri Tielemans and Andrey Santos. Despite spending massively, they have failed to produce the control they would have expected in the midfield. The lack of control was evident in the recent Tottenham game, where Mainoo-Tielemans partnership failed to control the game collectively.

The midfield question was covered in our recent report as well, highlighting a lack of control and carried comments from Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp.

The bigger question is when United will strengthen their defence. As per our previous report as well, Manchester United are planning to overhaul their defence in the upcoming transfer windows.

United were heavily linked with several left backs but eventually failed to bring any one. The situation left Luke Shaw as the only senior option in the position, while Patrick Dorgu is used upfront by Michael Carrick.

Harry Amass is another option for Michael Carrick, but he is only here because of a failed transfer move. He has been rarely used at left back as Dalot and Mazraoui get the nod in the position ahead of him.

The situation at right-back isn’t all good either. Diogo Dalot is the first option there, but he rarely provides the assurance that puts Michael Carrick at ease. Noussair Mazraoui is the second choice at right-back, but his attacking numbers aren’t great either.

Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez form up United’s centre-back pairing while Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven are used all over the defensive line in rotation when needed. Yoro was used at right-back against Tottenham while Heaven arrived as left-back towards the end of the game.

Manchester United’s season hasn’t been defensively strong or anywhere near being good. They have conceded goals in all Premier League games and only kept one clean sheet against Sabah in the Champions League.

Lisandro Martinez has come under scrutiny after errors on four occasions. Against Ipswich Town, Manchester United won 5-2, but his error resulted in Ipswich’s second goal.

His own goal against Fulham gave away two points at Craven Cottage in the 1-1 draw. Another error led to Solanke’s goal against Tottenham, when he took his eyes off the ball.

His injury concerns are another aspect, and maybe Michael Carrick needs to make a decision.

Manchester United’s success depended heavily on youth over the years. When pundits questioned them, Sir Alex Ferguson gave them the chance to prove them wrong. Alan Hansen made the famous quote “You can’t win anything with kids” on the BBC’s Match of the Day on August 19, 1995. United did exactly that, played youth and went on to win everything.

Michael Carrick needs to do exactly that. He needs to trust youth and allow them to make mistakes. Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven arrived as massive potentials, but they haven’t been given the license to express themselves, without fearing for their places. Both the youngsters have rarely started together or in a stretch of games. It is maybe time for them to take their chances.

Michael Carrick needs to keep Harry Maguire as a senior experienced figure around them. There is no doubt over Martinez’s ability, but he is not the answer to United’s long-term problem. United need to blend youth with experience as their identity moving forward. Lisandro Martinez perhaps isn’t the answer to United’s problem.

A defensive revival expected in the upcoming transfer windows needs to happen now. It must happen with chances to the Youth. Michael Carrick needs to be brave enough and throw the youngsters in the mix. What it will bring is a long-term solution. They need to experience the errors and pressures that will shape them in football.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United are reportedly looking at options beyond Michael Carrick after the club believes he is not the right option for United.

Former Germany manager Julian Nagelsmann is among the names linked with a possible move to Old Trafford if United

Carrick’s achievements last season bought him the permanent role, but the opening weeks of this campaign have left little room for further slip-ups.

#2149. Arsenal could expose Liverpool’s biggest weakness at Anfield

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal have spent years struggling to impose themselves at Anfield. However, Mikel Arteta could have an opportunity to change that record when his side face Liverpool on Sunday.

The Gunners have often struggled against the physicality of Liverpool on Merseyside. Since Jurgen Klopp took charge, Liverpool have built a reputation for being high pressing, fast, and making their opponents make mistakes.

However, looking at the latest stats, it seems that Liverpool are no longer as physical, meaning that Arsenal could target their weaknesses.

The disparity is stark between the two teams, with Liverpool having run the least distance of any Premier League side this season. Meanwhile, Arsenal are running the most.

If Arteta’s side can capitalise on this and create fast attacks from possession, then they can utilise this to their advantage.

Liverpool have shifted away from their high-octane style associated with much of Klopp’s reign. Arne Slot has slowly moved towards a more possession-based approach, focusing on keeping the ball.

Andoni Iraola looks keen to add more intensity to their pressing. The Reds have had some of the best pressing stats in the Premier League this season (Opta Analyst).

However, they are not getting the desired intensity from the system.

In addition to chasing the opposition when they have the ball, players must quickly recover, cut off passing lanes and return to their defensive positions.

This is where Arsenal might have an opportunity. If they can get past Liverpool’s first line of pressure, they may have space to push forward before the home side can get back into position.

Another area that the Gunners could target is Liverpool’s midfield.

Alexis MacAllister and Ryan Gravenberch are technically gifted and very composed when they have the ball. But neither are a true defensive midfielder, which raises questions over their ability to stop teams playing through the middle.

Arsenal have the players to exploit this. Declan Rice can put in an energetic, physical performance and drive the play forward. Myles Lewis-Skelly provides another option who can run with the ball into dangerous areas.

This can cause problems for the Liverpool midfield. Arsenal can try to disrupt Mac Allister and Gravenberch, not letting them set the tempo of the game.

If they win these individual battles, it can stop Liverpool from getting the ball control they want.

The task is certainly a tall one. Arsenal have lost their last 16 games at Anfield, including seven in Arteta’s reign.

Whilst that may be the case, the current Liverpool team are very different to the one Arsenal have faced on a regular basis during Klopp’s time in charge.

They certainly can’t expect to be outplayed, but they must take advantage of Liverpool’s failings.

Arsenal are currently second in the Premier League with 21 points from eight games. They will want to win here but will need to do more than just run them into the ground.

If Rice and Lewis-Skelly can push forward and pressurise them, forcing them to make mistakes, then Arteta could finally have his winning recipe at Anfield.

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Emi Martinez has revealed that his move to Chelsea helped him recover mentally from the pain of losing the World Cup final with Argentina.

The goalkeeper left Aston Villa during the summer, with Chelsea confirming a contract until 2029.

He has now explained the personal motivation behind changing clubs.

Speaking after Argentina’s win over Burkina Faso, Martinez discussed his response to the World Cup disappointment in comments reported by TyC Sports.

“I tried changing clubs to refresh myself mentally, and I succeeded.”

Martinez was answering a question about an earlier social-media post that had raised doubts over his Argentina future. He explained that he now felt reinvigorated and ready to continue representing his country.

His explanation gives supporters a clearer understanding of a transfer that formed part of Chelsea’s summer goalkeeping changes.

Those changes also included Filip Jorgensen’s loan move to Strasbourg, as Xabi Alonso reshaped his options.

Martinez’s international break has since brought another positive moment. He received an ovation during the Argentina victory in which Chelsea team-mate Valentin Barco supplied the seventh goal.

#2147. Man Utd told £50m offer could tempt Fulham to sell highly rated teenager

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United could face an expensive battle to sign Fulham youngster Josh King, with offers exceeding £50million potentially enough to persuade the London club to consider a sale.

The 19-year-old has attracted attention with his performances this season, despite Fulham’s difficult results.

Manchester United are among several clubs monitoring his progress, alongside Liverpool, Arsenal, Borussia Dortmund and Barcelona.

Speaking on the Transfer Insider podcast, Pete O’Rourke explained that King’s growing reputation has put him on the radar of several major clubs.

“Josh King will be on the radar for a number of clubs because of his performances for Fulham so far this season,” O’Rourke said.

“It’s not just Liverpool, there’s been talk of the likes of Manchester United, Arsenal, Borussia Dortmund and even Barcelona looking at Josh King.”

Fulham are not actively planning to let the teenager leave in the near future as he is under contract until 2029. The contract length gives Fulham an upper hand, while interest from multiple clubs indicates a concession is not expected.

“Fulham are not planning for any early departure for Josh King. He is still under contract until 2029, and obviously they’ll want to keep him.”

The reporter also pointed to Fulham’s league position as a factor that could influence their decision. If they become embroiled in a relegation battle, losing one of their best performers would be a major risk.

“If Manchester United or any other club wanted to make an offer, he’s going to be worth in excess of £50million probably right now.”

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Morgan Rogers struggled to establish himself at Bournemouth because the role envisaged for him did not fit the team’s usual setup, according to former Cherries scout Des Taylor.

Chelsea’s £117million signing faces his former club on Saturday, with Taylor offering fresh insight into a difficult period in the attacker’s development.

Speaking to BBC Sport’s Nizaar Kinsella in a report syndicated by AOL, Taylor explained:

“We saw him as a number 10, but Parker was not really playing with a number 10.”

Rogers started only three matches and scored once during the loan under Scott Parker.

It adds a specific explanation to the broader setbacks Rogers discussed in our earlier coverage of the experiences that shaped his Chelsea move.

Rogers approaches the reunion after scoring England’s seventh goal against Croatia, finishing after rounding the goalkeeper.

#2145. Arsenal news: Arsenal vs Liverpool. Arteta targets Anfield record as Liverpool prepare for Premier League clash

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal vs Liverpool: Arsenal travel to Anfield on Sunday looking to maintain their strong start to the Premier League season and end a long wait for victory at Liverpool’s home ground.

A blistering start to the season has seen Mikel Arteta’s side win seven of their first eight league games, leaving them tied on points with Manchester City at the top of the table and six points ahead of Liverpool going into the game.

However, the Gunners have not beaten Liverpool in the Premier League at Anfield since 2012/13. Arteta will be aware that the Reds are starting to gel under Andoni Iraola.

They have won three of their last four league games after a lacklustre start and had their first league win at home this season with their 2-0 win over Brighton.

After replacing Arne Slot over the summer, Iraola brought his impressive three-year spell at Bournemouth to Anfield. However, Liverpool have struggled to adjust to the new manager, losing 3 of their first 4 league games.

But they have recently improved and are looking to play a much faster style of football. As Adrian Clarke notes, they are pressing more, not sitting back as much and wanting to attack quickly when they win the ball.

They have been the most direct attackers in the Premier League. They have pace to spare within their attack, with players like Alexander Isak and Cody Gakpo providing a threat in advanced areas.

Isak comes into the game in good form after finding the back of the net six times in his first eight league games. He has scored in three successive games in the Premier League for the first time since joining the club.

Florian Wirtz will also play a key role in Liverpool’s attacking play. The German has been consistently creating chances for the team playing behind Isak in a 4-2-3-1 system. He has created nine chances against Brentford.

Arsenal will need to limit the space available to Liverpool’s forward players. Allowing Isak and Gakpo to combine freely could make an already difficult away fixture even harder.

Arsenal have had some positive news on their squad ahead of their Merseyside trip. Declan Rice and Riccardo Calafiori are both back in training.

Christos Tzolis has also trained after missing October. Noni Madueke has also not been ruled out by Arteta ahead of Sunday’s match after he missed the Fleetwood game.

They also have their own fitness issues to contend with. Iraola confirmed that Dominik Szoboszlai and Victor Munoz are both available after suffering injuries earlier in the month.

Jeramie Frimpong will be missing due to a hamstring issue but should be back after the international break. Hugo Ekitike, Geovanni Leoni and Conor Bradley are all still sidelined with longer-term injuries.

The presence of Rice will be key for Arsenal. He has been one of the best progressive midfielders in the Premier League, along with their captain, Martin Odegaard.

Despite losing just one of their last eight Premier League clashes against Liverpool, Arsenal are not as strong on the road.

The Gunners have not won any league games in their last 13 trips to Anfield. They have drawn five and lost eight. Their last win was a 2-0 victory back in the 2012/13 season.

They have also never gone 14 games unbeaten away from home in the league. That would be a great achievement.

However, there is good reason to believe they can do it. They have only conceded five clean sheets in the league this season. They have faced the least number of shots on target and the fewest expected goals.

Liverpool have also been on a strong run defensively with four clean sheets in their last six league games, so Arsenal must make their chances count.

A win would provide another key result against a direct rival, improving their position at the top of the Premier League table and finally putting an end to one of the clubs’ longest-running away day woes.

#2144. Why must Manchester United move beyond the Glazers?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Glazers took control of Manchester United in 2005. That takeover has since caused so many disruptions and protests by the fans over massive debts and insufficient backing of the team in the transfer market, despite successful periods in the Premier League and Europe.

Now Manchester United need to think about who should control the club in the future. Laurie Whitwell has now shared his views on why Manchester United need an ownership change. He simply wants the Glazers gone from Old Trafford. However, he also recognizes that replacing the current ownership doesn’t automatically mean things will improve for United.

The journalist believes the change in ownership has had a lasting effect on United, with the club still feeling its consequences today. A fresh start might be welcome, but the identity of the next owners would matter just as much as the departure of the existing ones.

“Personally, I would like to see the Glazers gone from Manchester United,” Laurie Whitwell said on The Rest is Football podcast with Gary Lineker.

“That change, you know, just really affected and still impacts United now.”

The desire for different ownership is one thing, but finding people capable of taking the club forward is another.

Whitwell admitted there is no perfect answer when it comes to who should take charge.

That leaves United facing a difficult choice. A sale could offer the chance to change how the club is run, but simply bringing in a new owner would not guarantee better decisions or a brighter future.

Whitwell also raised the idea of United supporters having a greater say in the club’s ownership and decisions, much like the Barcelona model where fans own the club itself. However, he highlighted that the Barcelona model isn’t ideal either and has its own shortcomings.

Changing the names at the top would only be one part of the change. The club would also need an ownership structure capable of making sound decisions and providing a direction.

The issue of ownership goes beyond whether the Glazers should stay or leave. Manchester United needs to find the right people and the right model to secure the club’s future. Getting that decision wrong could leave them facing the same circus the fans are enduring under the Glazers.

#2143. Sheffield United must plot Coventry City transfer action after major Chris Wilder reveal

Sheffield United’s positive run of form came to an end as they suffered a 2-1 defeat at struggling Charlton Athletic last time out.

After an inconsistent start to the campaign, Chris Wilder’s side looked as though they had turned a corner after going four games unbeaten, which included recording three wins.

So, they went to The Valley full of confidence, but despite Jordan Ayew cancelling out Charlton’s opener, the hosts would pick up all three points after Matty Godden’s late strike.

That meant the Blades failed to move into the play-off places, although it can’t detract from the general improvements they have made in the last month or so.

Unfortunately for Wilder and Sheffield United, the defeat in the capital came at a further cost, as Femi Seriki was forced off just minutes after replacing Hamza Chouhury at half-time.

The 24-year-old had already missed a series of games with a knee injury, and Wilder has confirmed that Seriki has now suffered a patella tendon rupture, which means he won’t return until the 2027/28 season.

In many ways, that overshadowed the result for the Blades, as Seriki is a very talented player, and right-back has been a problem position for the Yorkshire outfit.

Choudhury has filled in that position since he joined from Leicester in the summer, and he can do a job, but it’s not his natural position. Plus, at times, he looks vulnerable when he comes up against quick wingers who want to take him on.

Matt Doherty is another option, but the 34-year-old has fitness issues of his own, and he hasn’t convinced in the limited time he has had on the pitch since becoming a Blade in the summer.

Therefore, Seriki’s injury is surely going to force Sheffield United into the market for a new right-back, as Wilder will want a new recruit for that role.

And, one player who could be a shrewd addition is Coventry’s Kaine Kesler-Hayden.

The form of Milan van Ewijk means Kesler-Hayden is second choice for the Sky Blues, and the reality is that, providing the Dutchman stays fit, it’s hard to see that changing any time soon.

So, a loan deal seems possible, which would obviously suit Sheffield United given their financial situation, and, from a football perspective, Kesler-Hayden has qualities that Wilder will love.

The former Aston Villa man plays with intensity, he loves to get forward, and he is also capable of defending in a one-v-one.

In the 2024/25 season, Kesler-Hayden was outstanding on loan at Preston, so there will also be few doubts about his ability to cope in the second tier, and it was that form that prompted Coventry to splash out around £5m on the player, at a time when van Ewijk was expected to leave.

FLW looks at which of the division's 24 clubs are the biggest and lowest spenders

So, this is a player with serious pedigree by Championship standards, but there’s no denying that Kesler-Hayden’s career has stalled.

Even though he contributed to Coventry’s title win last season, featuring in 22 games, the right-back only played 585 minutes.

An injury meant he missed parts of this season, but his only outing was as an 87th-minute substitute at Spurs.

Clearly, he won’t be enjoying that, and whilst Lampard would want to keep Kesler-Hayden around, he will recognise that the past 18 months haven’t played out as he envisaged, so it wouldn’t be a shock if he agreed to let the 24-year-old leave for the sake of his career.

Pick any two players. Send the code, and your mate enters it here to play the same pair.

As outlined above, the Seriki news is likely to change the Blades’ transfer plans, but they aren’t going to be working with the biggest budget.

Obviously, that could make deals harder to do, and they may have to wait to see what sort of players become available.

But, the recruitment team will be working on targets, and, in an ideal world, they will be able to secure a few key signings as early as possible.

There’s still a lot of football to be played between now and January, so the outlook at Bramall Lane could change, but it seems likely that they will be among a host of clubs aiming to finish in the top eight.

Which position must Sheffield United prioritise in January? Let us know in the comments below.

Yet, there will be major concerns about whether Wilder’s current squad will last the distance, so a few new faces are needed to beef up the group and to add more quality.

Kesler-Hayden would be the sort of signing that could benefit Sheffield United, and they must ask the question of Coventry heading into the New Year.

#2142. Arsenal injury news: Arteta faces selection dilemma ahead of Liverpool clash

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal will have a few key injury updates ahead of their clash with Liverpool on Sunday, but Mikel Arteta still has some tough choices to make in his front line.

Declan Rice, Riccardo Calafiori and Christos Tzolis have returned to training so there is hope they can play in the Premier League clash against Anfield.

Bukayo Saka is still out with a quad injury, but Noni Madueke looks to be fit to go into the final game. Max Dowman is also suspended after Arsenal were unable to overturn his red card suspension after the defeat to Fleetwood in the Carabao Cup.

However, Arteta has some good options to choose from. He can ill afford any more injuries in attack.

Rice’s return will be key after missing the midweek cup clash.

The midfielder was subbed in the win against Everton with a knock. His absence was a blow against Fleetwood.

He trained on Friday. As long as he does another session on Saturday, he will be available. Rice is a big boost for Arsenal in the middle of the park as they face a Liverpool side who have been in good form recently.

Calafiori is also edging closer to a return. The Italian has played in the last seven Premier League games for Arsenal but missed against Everton.

However, he was only on for a short period when he came on as a sub. But he seemed to have an issue. Arteta said that it didn’t seem like a big problem, but they’re taking some caution.

Like Rice, Calafiori trained on Friday and will be able to go to Merseyside if he finishes his session on Saturday.

Tzolis is another possible addition. The winger has missed Arsenal’s last five matches after suffering a hamstring injury while playing for Greece in October.

His latest scan showed encouraging progress, and he has now returned to training. A further successful session would put him in contention for Sunday’s match.

The main issue for Arteta will be his lack of attacking width.

Madueke did not join the squad for the trip to Fleetwood due to an ankle injury sustained in the Everton game. He did not train on Monday, but Arteta has not ruled him out of the game against Liverpool. He will have to finish his session on Saturday.

Dowman is also missing due to suspension. He was sent off in the 1-1 draw against Fleetwood after a challenge from Harrison Neal. His appeal was rejected by the FA, so he will miss the game against Liverpool.

Saka makes it even worse for the Gunners. The England international has a quad injury from the Bayern Munich game and is likely to be out for several weeks.

Arteta confirmed that he will need another scan before there is a clearer timeline for his return but is likely to be out for a long period of time.

William Saliba is still missing from the squad, having aggravated a back injury during his time playing for France at the World Cup this summer.

But the defender has returned to doing one-on-one training on the training ground.

Arsenal will perform an MRI scan to monitor his progress. Arteta has suggested there have been positive signs, and he is expected back in December.

The main talking point will be Rice, Calafiori and Tzolis returning to training ahead of Sunday.

It would give Arsenal a nice boost with all three players fit. Madueke is still a doubt and they have fewer options going forward with Saka and Dowman out.

#2140. Fabrizio Romano shares latest on Martinez contract after explosive rant

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Argentinian defender has also apologized to the United higher ups as well over his comments, as shared by Fabrizio Romano.

Lisandro Martinez has held discussions with Jason Wilcox and Michael Carrick after speaking out following United’s 2-0 defeat to Atletico Madrid.

Martinez had questioned whether Carrick was backed enough in the summer and suggested the club needed changes.

Fabrizio Romano has now confirmed that United consider the matter resolved for now and expect Martinez to remain available for selection.

Speaking about the situation on his YouTube channel, as quoted by Football 365, Romano explained why Martinez’s comments caused concern inside the club.

“He spoke very strongly after the game against Atletico Madrid, which Man Utd lost. It was a very negative moment for the Red Devils. We know that Licha was very strong in his comments. He said that Michael Carrick was not backed enough during the summer transfer window.”

Romano added that Martinez had spoken directly to those at the club after the incident.

“Licha apologised to Man Utd as a club. He spoke to the directors, he spoke to Jason Wilcox, and he spoke to Michael Carrick, who was also involved in this conversation.”

“So, the underlying message remains the same. Lisandro is apologising for the way he expressed himself after a game during a difficult moment for Man Utd, but there was a discussion about the substance of what he said.”

Romano also revealed that United had imposed a financial penalty on the centre-back. However, the punishment does not appear to involve removing him from the squad.

“According to the information available tonight, Lisandro Martinez has been fined.”

He added that Martinez is expected to return to the squad as normal, with the fine considered sufficient punishment for the incident.

“The internal discussion also helped to resolve all the issues. For Man Utd, the case is closed.”

That should bring some immediate clarity to the situation, although questions remain about how the episode could affect Martinez’s longer-term position at Old Trafford.

Romano also pointed towards Martinez’s contract, which expires next summer, with United holding an option to extend it.

“So, let’s see what is going to happen there. For sure, this is the kind of situation that will have an impact on the situation surrounding Lisandro, but we have to see what happens next in terms of future transfers and everything else.”

#2138. Will Lisandro Martinez play against Leeds after explosive rant?

“I am told that Martinez is available for selection this weekend, and there is every chance Michael Carrick starts him,” Bailey said.

The defender recently found himself in trouble after questioning whether Manchester United had done enough in the summer transfer window. He apologised to the club’s hierarchy and was fined, but the incident has not apparently created a divide between him and the manager.

“Martinez was not critical of Carrick – far from it. This isn’t a repeat of something like Mo Salah criticising Arne Slot last season. It is almost the opposite.”

Bailey went on to suggest that the fallout may have strengthened the bond between the players and their manager.

Arsenal are increasing their interest in Cruzeiro youngster Eduardo Pape.

Arsenal are reportedly targeting Pape as a potential signing in 2027. The teenager is valued at around £33m.

The contract is until December 2029, meaning Cruzeiro are in a strong position. There isn’t a huge need for the Brazilian club to rush into any decisions, meaning Pape can get some experience in a comfortable environment before moving abroad.

However, they have been very successful in the past at bringing in young Brazilian players. Gabriel Martinelli was brought in from Ituano as a teenager and has become an important first team player.

It gives us an insight into what Pape could hope to achieve.

It shows that Arsenal are looking further ahead with their recruitment. Whether they make a move for him remains to be seen. Pape will be one to keep an eye on as we approach the 2027 transfer window.

Arsenal are keeping their options open regarding a January move for Real Madrid striker Endrick, as Mikel Arteta looks to strengthen his attacking options.

The Gunners have been in contact about a loan move for the Brazilian. There is also talk of a permanent move. However, Real Madrid are reluctant to let him leave and feel he has a big future ahead at the club.

Endrick has struggled to earn consistent game time at the Bernabeu.

While he is only 20 years old and has plenty of time left to make his mark at the top level, a new transfer away from Madrid could give him a more consistent role.

Arsenal are among the clubs keeping an eye on Endrick ahead of the January transfer window, reports TEAMtalk.

The forward was loaned to Lyon for the second half of last season to get more regular game time.

However, his representatives are said to be open to looking for another move if things do not improve in Madrid.

It seems Arsenal are interested in signing Endrick on loan with a view to making the move permanent. This will give them time to see how he settles into the Premier League.

However, Real Madrid may be in a strong position. They are still loyal to the young player and would prefer to keep him at the Spanish club.

Arsenal will rely on Madrid being more willing to let him go.

The reported interest from Arsenal comes as Arteta looks to strengthen his attacking options.

Endrick would provide variety to an attacking line-up which has been under pressure of late. His age and potential make a loan deal with an option to make the move permanent an attractive option.

Arsenal have previously signed young talents from Real Madrid.

Endrick will be hoping to follow a similar trajectory.

There is no suggestion that any deal is near despite Arsenal’s interest.

Endrick remains an important part of Real Madrid’s long-term plans. His representatives are thought to be looking at other options to help his career develop.

This loan deal would provide a solution for all parties. Arsenal would have another attacker to bring in, Endrick would play more regularly and Madrid would still have an interest in his future.

It will also depend on how willing the Spanish giants are to move their stance before January.

For now, Arsenal are in contact with Endrick as they consider a move. The Brazilian’s future may soon become a topic of interest ahead of the winter transfer window.

#2132. Charlton Athletic can forget about Rak-Sakyi by sealing Man City transfer

Charlton Athletic head into the international break with something to cheer for after an abysmal run of form saw the Addicks plummet down the Championship table, as head coach Nathan Jones will surely be preparing plans for the January transfer window.

Having defeated each of Preston North End, West Ham United, and Derby County in their opening three Championship games of the season, it looked as though the Addicks could be surprise play-off contenders this year.

However, an 11-game winless run would ensue, as Charlton subsequently tumbled down the second-tier table, and as they hosted Sheffield United on Saturday afternoon, it appeared as a must-win game for head coach Nathan Jones.

The Welsh boss indeed claimed all three points, but on the whole, he will surely be disappointed with the way things have panned out in the early stages of the season, and may well be thinking things could have gone differently had they sealed one signing in particular.

After a busy summer in 2025 that saw the likes of Charlie Kelman, Thomas Kaminski, Harvey Knibbs, and Rob Apter head to The Valley, Charlton endured a much less lucrative window this time around.

Ivan Mesik, Millenic Alli, Athur Okonkwo, Tyler Bindon, and Karlan Grant headed to the English capital to link up with Jones' side, though in the final stages of the window, Charlton were eager to complete a deal to bring former winger Jesurun Rak-Sakyi back to the club.

Rak-Sakyi, 24, spent the 2022/23 campaign on loan with the club in League One from Crystal Palace, making 43 league appearances for the Addicks, scoring 15 and assisting eight, shooting himself into prominence.

A deal worth in excess of £4 million had been agreed between Charlton and Palace to bring the Ghanaian back to the club, but the two clubs were unable to agree on the final details of the deal, as Rak-Sakyi headed to Turkish outfit Kasimpasa on loan instead.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top 25 most demanding fanbases in British football right now

Having missed out on Rak-Sakyi, Charlton have more often than not looked to Tyreece Campbell or Karlan Grant to fill the void on the right flank, and though neither have done a poor job, both are better off the left where they can cut in on their preferred right foot.

Alli can also play on the right, though aside from the Irishman, Charlton are not exactly blessed with a plethora of wide options, as Jones should look to remedy this with a January switch for out-of-favour Swansea City winger Jeremy Monga.

Monga, 17, came through the ranks of Leicester City, and following the Foxes' relegation to League One last season, joined Manchester City in a deal worth around £10 million in the summer, before immediately heading out on loan to Swansea City.

However, his move to South Wales has not gone as planned, as now, according to the Swansea Independent, talks between Swansea and Man City have taken place to end his loan spell early, as Charlton must be on red-hot alert regarding this situation.

Upon his move to Swansea, it was hoped that Monga would provide strong competition for the likes of Joseph Opoku, Eom Ji-sung, Ronald, and Moussa Yeo, though that has proven to be far from the case right now.

Portuguese head coach Vitor Matos has been incredibly reluctant to use Monga at all, with the teenager appearing on the bench just three times and yet to earn a single minute of first-team football in South Wales.

Unsurprisingly, Man City are keen to accelerate his development in the EFL, and his lack of game time at Swansea means that he is almost certain to be on the lookout for another club come January, as Charlton must throw their hat into the mix.

The Addicks have played a role in the development of the likes of Jeremy Sarmiento, Alfie Doughty, Largie Ramazani, Omari Hutchinson, and Ademola Lookman over the years, and with that sort of record, should use this evidence to entice Monga to The Valley this winter.

Would you like to see Monga at your club? Why hasn't it quite worked out for the youngster at Swansea? Can he thrive in the Championship right now? Share your thoughts below!

Amid Charlton's struggles, Jones must have been scratching his head, wondering exactly how to get a tune out of his current crop of players, and no doubt will already have one eye on the January window.

Under increased pressure, Charlton's victory over Sheffield United most recently has likely bought him some more time, as this two-week break will provide the Welshman with the breathing space to amend things at The Valley.

With Monga looking almost certain to return from his loan at Swansea, Charlton must move quickly to secure his signature, as the teenager has the potential to play a crucial role for the Addicks for the remainder of the Championship campaign.

Speaking to former team-mate John Obi Mikel on The Obi One Podcast, the Brazilian revisited the decision that ended his seven-year Stamford Bridge spell in 2020.

Chelsea offered two more years, while Willian wanted three. His contract-length disagreement with Chelsea featured in ReadChelsea’s coverage at the time.

Willian says the reality differed from the picture presented during talks. Within three months, he had told his agent he wanted to leave.

His Arsenal spell ultimately lasted one season. He scored once in 37 appearances before leaving for Corinthians in 2021.

#2129. Arsenal news: Arteta’s side secure landmark victory at Anfield

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

In Arsenal news, Arsenal have finally found a way to win at Anfield again. Their latest victory owed as much to defensive discipline as Kai Havertz’s decisive goal.

The Gunners claimed a 1-0 Premier League victory against Liverpool, ending their 14-year wait for victory at the iconic stadium. Declan Rice set up their opener as Havertz put the visitors ahead on the 16th minute.

It was a tough afternoon for Mikel Arteta’s side as they were put under sustained pressure by their opponents. The Reds also created a number of dangerous chances throughout the match, especially in the first half.

Arsenal held their composure, and the backline was calm throughout. David Raya made the crucial interventions to keep the lead safe.

They are now three points ahead at the top of the Premier League after Manchester City lost to Brighton.

While the attack has been a key aspect of Arsenal’s title charge, they were put under pressure defensively at Anfield.

Liverpool came out aggressively after going behind. Virgil van Dijk caused problems from set pieces, and Florian Wirtz hit the crossbar from a free kick.

Raya was quick to block Van Dijk’s dangerous flick on and prevent Alexander Isak from capitalising. However, Alexis Mac Allister did head over the bar as Liverpool looked to level the game.

Arsenal were forced into another stretch as Jurrien Timber blocked well. William Saliba and Gabriel helped to limit Liverpool’s chances.

The stats tell the story of how well the visitors defended. Liverpool had 17 shots but couldn’t get one on target.

Liverpool were unable to create a shot on target for the first time in recorded history (since 2003-04) in a Premier League game at Anfield.

Meanwhile, it was the first time that Arsenal had kept a clean sheet in the Premier League at the ground since September 2012. The result was especially important following their Carabao Cup heartbreak against Fleetwood Town earlier in the week.

Whilst they have made a great defensive stand, Arsenal’s two most influential players in the final third made the difference.

Rice won the turnover, drove down the right and played a low cross into the six-yard box. Havertz made his run to the back post and finished to give Arsenal the early lead.

The goal also takes Havertz to 46 Premier League goals, making him the most prolific German in the competition and overtaking former Manchester City midfielder Ilkay Gündogan.

Rice had a huge influence on the game. His performance in the middle of the park helped Arsenal deal with the intensity of Liverpool, and he was named man of the match.

Christos Tzolis provided an important attacking outlet. His pace created chances to ease pressure on Arsenal. He forced an incredible save from Alisson with a late first-time effort.

The win was Arsenal’s first at Anfield in any competition since September 2012 and their 300th away win in the Premier League.

Arteta’s team have had a stellar start to the season, taking 24 points from nine games. They have also proved they are capable of getting results in tough circumstances.

They will now look ahead to their Champions League game on Wednesday against Slavia Prague and then back to domestic action against Hull City.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United had a difficult evening once again as they were held to a 1-1 draw at Leeds United. Pressure mounts on Michael Carrick as United have just 7 points from 7 games.

Manchester United took the lead through Bryan Mbeumo in the first half, with the forward finishing Youri Tielemans’ excellent cross from the right. However, the visitors couldn’t hold on to their advantage as Dominic Calvert-Lewin equalised for Leeds United from a corner.

The draw extended United’s frustrating run of results, leaving Michael Carrick still searching for the consistency his side need.

Carragher, though, believes there were positives to take from the trip, particularly one performance at the back.

Jamie Carragher has singled out Harry Maguire for praise after Manchester United drew 1-1 with Leeds United at Elland Road on Sunday.

Speaking on Sky Sports, the former Liverpool defender admitted that picking up a point at Elland Road was not necessarily a bad result in isolation, even if United’s recent form made it feel like a missed opportunity.

“Going away to Leeds is going to be tricky for any team except for Manchester City and Arsenal at the moment,” Carragher said.

“A lot of teams will take a point at Leeds this season but the problem for Manchester United is the results have been so poor that they need to start winning games so it almost feels like two points dropped.”

Carragher then turned his attention to Maguire, who he felt played a major role in keeping United in the game when Leeds began to push for a winner.

“But in isolation, getting a point at Leeds is a decent result. I thought Harry Maguire was fantastic, especially in the second half.”

Leeds United grew into the contest after Calvert-Lewin brought them level, putting United under pressure during the closing stages. Carragher felt Maguire’s efforts in defence helped prevent the visitors from falling apart.

“After Leeds got that goal back there was a stage of the game where it felt like Man United could go under, but Maguire at the back held it together for them. He was fantastic.”

The result may feel like another opportunity missed for Carrick, but Maguire’s performance gave United something positive to take away from Elland Road.
